<meta>This page continues the exploration of divine nomenclature, shifting from the internal permutations of the Name Y-H-W-H to how other names—such as El and Elohim—act as garments or attributes through which the Creator interacts with the world. It establishes the hierarchy of these names within the structure of the Divine Chariot.</meta>

### Page 31

The names such as **El**, **Elohim**, **Shaddai**, **Tzvaot** <note>God, God (plural form), Almighty, and Hosts</note>, and those similar to them: in which attribute does He stand, or in what garment does He clothe Himself? When one calls upon one of the names, such as **El**, **Elohim**, **Ele** <note>These</note>, or **Shaddai**, and their like, you must know who the camps and hosts of **El** are, or who the camps and hosts of **Elohim** are. In this way, you will understand that all the names and the epithets <note>Hebrew: *Kinnuyin*; descriptive titles like "The Gracious One"</note> are entirely dependent on the Name of the Lord <note>Y-H-W-H</note>, may He be blessed.

**And after** you have known this, you must know what the Attribute of Mercy is and what the Attribute of Judgment <note>Hebrew: *Din*</note> is; or the attribute of Judge, or the attribute of Arbiter; or the attribute of "Forgiver of Iniquity" and "He who passes over transgression"; or from which attribute comes the Fire, or the attribute called Water, or the attribute of Spirit. What is the action of all these attributes, or how do these attributes intermingle with one another? All these matters are included within the epithets, and the epithets are included within the Holy Names, and the Holy Names are included within the Name **Y-H-W-H**, may He be blessed. He is called sometimes by this epithet and sometimes by that epithet, and it is all according to the attribute with which He conducts His world. As you will find written: *"Gracious and merciful is the Lord..."* and so on; and it says, *"And You, O Lord, are on high forever,"* and *"O Lord, our Lord, how majestic is Your name in all the earth!"* and so with all similar verses. Therefore, a person must know how all the names and epithets are carried by the great Name **Y-H-W-H**, may He be blessed. When you understand this, then you will know and understand that you are a lowly creature before the Perfect in Knowledge <note>Hebrew: *Tamim De'im*; a reference to God from Job 37:16</note>, may He be blessed.

**Know** that these three names—**El**, **Elohim**, and **Y-H-W-H**—are the knot of the entire Chariot <term>Merkavah|The "Chariot" refers to the Divine realm and the structure of the celestial hierarchy as seen in the visions of Ezekiel</term>. All the systems and all the hosts of the world, both upper and lower, are carried by them. One of them is the root—that is the Name **Y-H-W-H**—and the other two are carried by it; they are **El** and **Elohim**. **El** is to His right, and **Elohim** is to His left. This is the secret of the verse:

---

*"And all the host of heaven standing by Him on His right hand and on His left."* <note>1 Kings 22:19</note> All of them together are carried by the Name of the Lord, blessed be He. This is the secret of "on the right" for merit, and "on the left" for liability; the attribute of **El** is for mercy and merit, and the attribute of **Elohim** is for judgment and liability.

**And after** we have informed you of this, we must enter and explain the secret of the epithets hinted at in the beginning of the Chariot of Ezekiel: *"And it came to pass in the thirtieth year..."* <note>Ezekiel 1:1</note> These are the secret of the **thirty epithets** that support the Chariot. When the Name, may He be blessed, judges all His creatures for good or for evil—to reward with good, or to punish, or for death, or for life—it is according to what the world deserves. Even though His judgments, may His name be blessed, are all built upon mercy, nevertheless, at the finalization of the verdict, He does not waive anything. Even though He is long-suffering and waits, in the end, He finalizes the judgment. This is what our Sages of blessed memory said: *"Whoever says the Merciful One is a waiver [of justice], his life shall be waived"* <note>original: "כל מאן דאמר רחמנא וותרן הוא מוותרין לו חייו"; Babylonian Talmud, Bava Kamma 50a</note>; rather, He is long-suffering, but He collects His due. Therefore, you will need to know that each of these three names—**El**, **Elohim**, and **Y-H-W-H**—carries the epithets as one who clothes himself. Thus, everything is found to be epithets, and they all depend on the Name, may He be blessed. All these epithets are called "wings," as it is hinted: *"Spread your wings over your handmaid..."* <note>Ruth 3:9</note> When the Holy One judges all His creatures according to the judgment that is fitting before Him, it is through those thirty epithets. And these are the "variations" <note>Hebrew: *Shinuyin*, playing on the word for epithets/kinnuyin</note> by which creatures vary from one another, whether for good or for evil. You find creatures categorized by them: for example, how this righteous person is different from that wicked person. Just as this one is different from that one, so are their judgments different from one another. According to the variations among human beings, so are the variations in the judgments they deserve. And according to the variations in the deserving judgments, so are the variations found in the thirty epithets that cause the judgment. For instance, "Merciful" enacts His judgment and finishes it for good; "Man of War" finishes His judgment for punishment; "Truth" finishes His judgment for both mercy and punishment. And according to...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of divine names and attributes, specifically focusing on how the Tetragrammaton—the four-letter Name of God—interacts with and "wears" the attributes of Mercy (represented by the name El) and Judgment (represented by the name Elohim). It transitions from the general concept of divine garments to the specific roles of the Patriarchs in this celestial hierarchy.</meta>

In the epithet called **El** and the secret of **El**:
A King sitting upon a throne of mercy, and this is the secret of the verse, *"For the Lord your God is a merciful God"* <note>Hebrew: *El Rachum*; Deuteronomy 4:31</note>. And the sign for this is: *"For the Lord is a great God"* <note>Hebrew: *El Gadol*; Psalm 95:3</note> *"and a great King above all gods."* The interpretation of "the Cherub" <note>Hebrew: *Ha-Keruv*</note> is the Attribute of Mercy, a great King over all "gods"—where "gods" <note>Hebrew: *Elohim*</note> refers to the Attribute of Judgment. From here you may understand the secret of the verse, *"And God tested Abraham"* <note>Genesis 22:1</note>; for God <note>Hebrew: *Ha-Elohim*</note> placed the standard <note>Hebrew: *degel*; a banner or military division</note> in the hand of Abraham, whose own attribute is **El** <note>Kindness/Mercy</note>. This is the secret of the phrase, *"The God of Abraham, the God of Isaac, and the God of Jacob"* <note>Exodus 3:6</note>; it does not say "and the God of Isaac" <note>meaning, it repeats the title "God of" to separate the attributes</note> because the standard of Isaac—which is the attribute of **Elohim** <note>Judgment</note>—is held in the hand of Abraham, who is:

->The attribute of **El**:<-

**And when** the Name, may He be blessed, is angry at His world on account of their evil deeds, He then brings out the "vessels of His wrath" and clothes Himself in "garments of vengeance." He then repays every person of the world with that judgment which they deserve. The secret of this is, *"Let God arise, let His enemies be scattered"* <note>Psalm 68:2</note>, and the sign is, *"For judgment belongs to God"* <note>Deuteronomy 1:17</note>. At such a time, the form of the name **Elohim** <note>God of Judgment</note> is engraved upon the standard of the Tetragrammaton <note>Hebrew: *Y-H-W-H*; the core Name of Mercy</note>. This secret is hinted at in the verse, *"Rise up, O Lord, and let Your enemies be scattered"* <note>Numbers 10:35</note>, which corresponds to *"Let God arise, let His enemies be scattered."* If so, why does it say "the Lord" <note>Y-H-W-H</note> in one place and "God" <note>Elohim</note> in the other? I have already informed you that all the names are dependent upon the Name of the Lord, blessed be He, and He clothes Himself in them. When it says *"Rise up, O Lord,"* the Name of the Lord has taken up the vessels of His wrath, and **Elohim** is engraved upon His standard; this is the secret of *"Let God arise."* And when the Name, blessed be He, brings the people of the world into judgment, He wears neither garments of pure mercy nor garments of pure judgment. Rather, because He does not come with the attribute of absolute judgment nor with absolute mercy, He comes with the attribute of judgment mingled with mercy. The secret of this is found in the phrase *"Slow to anger and abundant in kindness"* <note>Exodus 34:6</note>. Nevertheless, since the verse says *"abundant in kindness,"* it testifies that there is a mixture of judgment; for if it were entirely kindness, why would the scripture say "abundant"? What do we care if it is a lot or a little? Rather, the majority is directed toward the judgment of kindness, while the minority is judgment. And when the Name, blessed be He, judges the world, He is sometimes called "the Lord" <note>Y-H-W-H</note> in judgment,

and sometimes He is called "God" <note>Elohim</note> in judgment. It is all according to the attribute with which the Name calculates, and according to what the world deserves. How so? The scripture says, *"For the Lord is our judge"* <note>Isaiah 33:22</note>, and it says, *"O Lord, correct me, but with justice"* <note>Jeremiah 10:24</note>, and it says, *"The Lord will enter into judgment"* <note>Isaiah 3:14</note>. Behold, here judgment is associated with "the Lord" <note>Y-H-W-H</note>. Yet sometimes judgment is associated with "God" <note>Elohim</note>, as it says: *"When God arose to judgment"* <note>Psalm 76:10</note>, and it says, *"For God is the judge"* <note>Psalm 75:8</note>. This secret depends on the depth of the legal proceeding; the Name, blessed be He, is called according to the final verdict of the trial. If the judgment is mostly mercy, [He is called the Lord], but if it leans toward the side of strict law, He is called **Elohim**. In this way, contemplate every place in the Torah where you find the Crowned Name, which is the Name of the Lord <note>Y-H-W-H</note>. When it is called alongside one of the other names, you will understand that the Name of the Lord is a garment, like specific gardens, according to what the hour is:

->Worthy:<-

**And know** that the other epithets <note>Hebrew: *kinnuyin*</note> which are not among the Holy Names <note>the seven names that cannot be erased according to Jewish law</note>—which are permitted to be erased—such as **Merciful**, **Gracious**, **Pious**, **Holy**, and their like: these are "vessels" for the Holy Names, which are forbidden to be erased. Through these epithets, the Holy Names perform their actions. For example: the Name appointed over the Standard of Kindness <note>Hebrew: *Chesed*</note> is **El**. The epithets that take the form of its "soldiers" are: **Merciful**, **Gracious**, **Pious**, **Slow to Anger**, **Abundant in Kindness**, **Holy**, **Forgiving**, and **Pardoning**. And the Name appointed over the Standard of War and Judgment <note>Hebrew: *Din*</note> is **Elohim**. The epithets that take the form of its "soldiers" are: **Mighty**, **Judge**, **Arbiter**, **Strong**, **Powerful**, **Man of War**, **Hero**, **Visiting Iniquity**, and **Requiting Recompense**. And the Name appointed over the judgment that is comprised of both Mercy and Judgment—the Lord God <note>Y-H-W-H Elohim</note>—is the Name called **the Tetragrammaton**, blessed be He. He possesses both types of epithets as the form of His soldiers, such as: **Bearer of Iniquity**, **He who passes over transgression**, **Truth**, **High**, **Lofty**, **Preserver of Kindness**, **Dweller of Eternity**, and **Holy**. Thus I have informed you of the general principle, and I shall now enlighten your eyes:

->In the Name, blessed be He:<-

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the Divine reveals or conceals His presence through various "garments" or Names. The previous page established that the Attribute of Mercy (the Name El) and the Attribute of Judgment (the Name Elohim) serve as different standards or banners held by the Patriarchs. This page explores what happens when God "undresses" from these titles to reveal His inner essence to Israel, and how the concealment of that essence leads to Exile.</meta>

When He <term>unites<note>Hebrew: *mit'yached*—a term often used in Kabbalah to describe the alignment or "oneness" of different divine aspects.</note></term> with Israel in truth, He removes some of His <term>epithets<note>Hebrew: *kinuyin*. These are secondary names for God (like "Merciful," "Great," or "Jealous") that describe His actions rather than His essence.</note></term> which are His garments. Then Israel sees the essence of **the Lord** <note>original: *Y-H-V-H* (the Tetragrammaton). Throughout this text, the author uses the abbreviation יד'וד.</note>, may He be blessed, more than any other nation. Nevertheless, He is still somewhat clothed in some of His garments and epithets. The reason for this is that the masses are not all worthy of reaching the absolute truth of the Name of **the Lord**, may He be blessed, and they cannot know the ultimate end of His essence.

But when the Name, may He be blessed, unites with the righteous, the pious, and the Patriarchs of the world in His true strength, He then removes all of His epithets from Himself. Then the Name of **the Lord** alone is exalted, and the Name of **the Lord** is found to be simple <note>Meaning "uncompounded" or "pure," without the "layers" of descriptions.</note>, stripped of all the epithets which are His garments, and He stands with the pious of Israel and their righteous ones.

This is the meaning of the verse: *"I have taken off my robe; how can I put it back on?"* <note>Song of Songs 5:3. In this mystical interpretation, the "robe" represents the divine attributes.</note> When the Name, may He be blessed, was angry with Israel, He said: *"I have taken off my robe"*—meaning all those epithets I possess—so that the <term>ministers of the nations<note>Hebrew: *sarei umot ha-olam*. In Jewish thought, every nation has a celestial guardian or "minister" that represents its power.</note></term> of the world would not be able to latch onto Me. Thus, I remained with Israel alone. 

Why then do you sin and wish for Me to return and wear those epithets? For if I wear them, the ministers of the nations will latch onto those epithets and lead you into exile. Therefore, He said: *"I have taken off my robe; how can I put it back on?"* I have stripped from myself the 30 epithets <note>The Hebrew word for "robe" (*Ketonet*) is linked here to a secret count of 30 divine names.</note> which are the secret of my "robe"; do you truly wish for Me to return and wear it? 

*"I have washed my feet"* from the filth of the land of the nations—for the earth is My footstool <note>Isaiah 66:1</note>. How could you cause Me to lead My **Presence** <note>Hebrew: *Shekhinah*—the indwelling, feminine aspect of God that accompanies Israel in exile.</note> with you into exile in the lands of the nations, where they will be defiled by impurity? This is the secret of *"I have washed my feet; how can I defile them?"* <note>Song of Songs 5:3</note> and the secret of *"The earth is my footstool."*

If so, consider this: when Israel went into exile in Babylon, the Name, may He be blessed, clothed Himself in epithets, which are the "robe." Then the nations latched onto Him. This is what the Scripture means when it says: *"And it came to pass in the thirtieth year..."* <note>Ezekiel 1:1</note>. Who is "He" <note>The Hebrew word *vayehi* (and it was) is often read homiletically to hint at God's presence.</note>? When the Name, may He be blessed, was clothed in other garments, then *"I was among the exiles"*—all of this refers to the exile.

But in the time to come, when the **Presence** returns to Her place, the Name, may He be blessed, will strip off all those garments, the epithets and the "wings." Then Israel will see the Name, beholding Him face to face. This is the secret of the verse: *"Your Teacher will no longer be winged"* <note>Isaiah 30:20. The Hebrew *yikanef* is usually translated as "hidden," but the author relates it to *kanaf* (wing).</note>. From that time onward, *"Your eyes shall see your Teacher."* That is to say, He will no longer clothe Himself in those epithets called "wings," with which the Name, may He be blessed, would cover Himself from Israel so they could not see Him. This is why it says, *"Your Teacher will no longer be winged."*

And this is the secret of: *"For eye to eye they shall see when the Lord returns to Zion."* <note>Isaiah 52:8</note>. And it says: *"On that day it shall be said, 'Behold, this is our God; we have waited for Him... this is the Lord!'"* <note>Isaiah 25:9</note>. Surely every single person will point to Him with a finger and say, *"This is the Lord!"* When will this be? At the time when He strips off the epithets and removes the "wings" so He is "winged" no more.

And how do we know that "wings" are things that hide and cover? As it is said: *"And under the firmament their wings were straight, one toward the other; every one had two which covered on this side, and every one had two which covered on that side, their bodies"* <note>Ezekiel 1:23</note>. And it is said: *"Six wings, six wings for each; with two he covered his face, and with two he covered his feet, and with two he did fly"* <note>Isaiah 6:2</note>. Flying and covering happen as one. Once the **Presence** departed and made for Herself "wings," She went away, and Israel did not see Her. And it is written: *"And He rode upon a cherub, and did fly"* <note>Psalm 18:10</note>. This is what is meant when it is said that the **Presence** traveled in ten journeys <note>A traditional teaching from the Talmud, Tractate Rosh Hashanah 31a, describing the gradual departure of God's presence from the Temple before its destruction.</note>, as is brought in the section on Rosh Hashanah.

### Regarding
what we have said, that the 
**Holy Names** are not to be erased: 
And if you should raise a difficulty and say: since the Holy Names are "garments," and when the Name, may He be blessed, is clothed in them He is hidden and concealed from Israel, why then are they not erased? Indeed, this is for the benefit of Israel and the greatness of **the Lord**, in His saying: *"I have taken off my robe; how can I put it back on?"*

### Know
that even though we say that the 
**Holy Names** are not erased, they are 
truly epithets that He strips off. However, they are not lost from the world. As it is said: *"He shall strip off the linen garments which he put on when he went into the holy place, and shall leave them there"* <note>Leviticus 16:23, referring to the High Priest on the Day of Atonement.</note> forever. So too...

It interprets the Exile as a period where God wears these garments, allowing foreign powers to latch onto Him, while promising a future redemption where God will strip away these "wings" and epithets to be seen "eye to eye."</summary>

--- Page 85 ---
<meta>This page continues the exploration of the "garments" of the Divine. While the previous page discussed the stripping of these garments for the Patriarchs, this page applies that concept to the destruction of Jerusalem and the unique relationship between the Tetragrammaton and the People of Israel.</meta>

Since there are three names—*El*, *Elohim*, and **the Lord**—and each one carries ten <term>epithets<note>Hebrew: *kinuyin*. In Kabbalistic thought, these are descriptive titles (like "Great," "Mighty," or "Merciful") that serve as "garments" or "veils" for the Divine essence.</note></term>, there are a total of thirty epithets through which created beings undergo change. Therefore, it is written: *"And it came to pass in the thirtieth year"* [Ezekiel 1:1]. Through these thirty epithets, Ezekiel the prophet, peace be upon him, saw that Israel had rebelled and sinned until no place of merit remained for Israel in the Heavenly Court among these thirty epithets—which are thirty variations—that might have spared Jerusalem from destruction. Instead, from the side of absolute judgment, and with the agreement of *El*, *Elohim*, and **the Lord**, and through their thirty epithets—all agreeing in a true judgment—Jerusalem was destroyed. 

This is the secret of the verse: *"And it came to pass in the thirtieth year"*—it was then that Jerusalem was destroyed. Jerusalem represents the "fourth attribute," which corresponds to the fourth letter of the Name, the letter *He*. Thus, "on the fifth of the month," the <term>Shekhinah<note>The Divine Presence, often viewed as the feminine or indwelling aspect of God.</note></term> (which is the letter *He* called *Adonai*) departed and went into exile. This is the secret of the verse: *"And I was in the midst of the exile"* [Ezekiel 1:1]. The "I" [the Divine Presence] literally went into exile. This is what our Sages, of blessed memory, meant when they said: "Beloved are Israel, for to every place they were exiled, the Divine Presence went with them."

## And Now •
We must enter into the explanation of each and every one of them [the epithets], or to which "family" each belongs. Then you will understand the "awe of the Lord," may He be blessed.

## And Before
Before we enter the explanation of these thirty epithets, we must know that **the Lord** [Y-H-V-H], may He be blessed, is the Name unique to Israel. All other epithets are like wings and <term>garments<note>The author uses the metaphor of clothing to explain how the infinite God interacts with the finite world; the nations of the world interact with the "clothing," but Israel has a direct connection to the "body" or essence.</note></term> that the other names cling to. Indeed, the seventy guardian princes of the nations of the world cling to these epithets and His garments, but the Name **the Lord** is unique to Israel alone. 

This is the secret of: *"For you are a holy people to the Lord your God; the Lord has chosen you to be His treasured people [Am Segulah] out of all the peoples on the face of the earth"* [Deuteronomy 7:6]. This is also what is meant by: *"For the Lord’s portion is His people"* [Deuteronomy 32:9]. This is the secret of: *"For all the nations walk each in the name of its god, but we will walk in the name of the Lord our God forever and ever"* [Micah 4:5].

---

And this is the secret of: *"And all the peoples of the earth shall see that the Name of the Lord is called upon you, and they shall be afraid of you"* [Deuteronomy 28:10]. The great secret was revealed by Joshua, peace be upon him, when he said: *"When the Canaanites and all the inhabitants of the land hear of it, they will surround us and cut off our name from the earth. And what will You do for Your great Name?"* [Joshua 7:9]. 

Consider this: the Name is unique to Israel alone. This is what is meant by: *"You have affirmed the Lord... and the Lord has affirmed you"* [Deuteronomy 26:17-18]. Thus, the Name **the Lord** is unique to Israel, and the name "Israel" is unique to the Name **the Lord**; they are inseparable. You have learned, then, that the Name **the Lord** is unique to Israel alone, while the other nations and their princes cling only to His garments. 

This is the secret of: *"This is Jerusalem; I have set her in the midst of the nations, with countries all around her"* [Ezekiel 5:5]. Now I shall explain the Name **the Lord** and His portion of the land, which is Jerusalem, and His portion of the nations, which is Israel. This is the secret of: *"For Your Name is called upon Your city and upon Your people"* [Daniel 9:19]. It is also written: *"For the Lord has chosen Zion; He has desired it for His habitation"* [Psalm 132:13]. And it says: *"Blessed be the Lord out of Zion, who dwells in Jerusalem. Hallelujah"* [Psalm 135:21]. *"Out of Zion, the perfection of beauty, God has shined"* [Psalm 50:2]—meaning, He shined out from Zion to dwell in Jerusalem. This is the secret of: *"For the Lord will not abandon His people for His great Name’s sake"* [1 Samuel 12:22]. This means that even if Israel are not righteous, He will still not abandon them. This is because they contain a portion of His great Name.

## And Know
Know that when the Name, may He be blessed, appears before the nations, He is seen by them only through His garments and coverings. But when the Name, may He be blessed, stands over all of Israel, He stands with the members of His own household and removes some of His garments—just as it is the way of a king to remove some of his garments when he is with his family. 

This is the secret of the commandment: *"Three times a year shall all your males appear..."* [Exodus 23:17]. Our Sages of blessed memory said: "Just as one comes to see, so he comes to be seen." They hinted at a great secret here, the <term>Secret of Appearance<note>Hebrew: *Sod HaRe'ayon*. Traditionally referring to the pilgrimage to the Temple, here it is used mystically to describe the direct, "unveiled" encounter between God and the Jewish people during the festivals.</note></term>. They said at the beginning of the tractates *Pe'ah* and *Chagigah* that "All are obligated in the appearance [at the Temple]." And the "Secret of Appearance" mentioned at the beginning of tractate *Pe'ah*—even though the Name...

<summary>This page explains that while Jerusalem's destruction was decreed by thirty divine epithets, the core Name of God (the Tetragrammaton) remains uniquely and eternally tied to the People of Israel and Jerusalem. It uses the metaphor of a king removing his robes at home to describe how God reveals His essence to Israel while remaining veiled to other nations.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of Ezekiel’s vision in the "thirtieth year," explaining how this number corresponds to the thirty divine epithets (garments) that govern the world. It transitions from the historical destruction of Jerusalem to the theological mechanics of how the Divine Name moves between Mercy and Judgment.</meta>

As our Sages of blessed memory said: "Behold, **the Lord** <note>original: יה"ו (*YHV*), a three-letter version of the four-letter Tetragrammaton, the essential Name of God</note> goes out from His place"—He goes out from the **Attribute of Mercy** <term>Middat ha-Rakhamim<note>The divine quality of compassion and grace.</note></term> and comes to the **Attribute of Judgment** <term>Middat ha-Din<note>The divine quality of strict justice and law.</note></term>. And what "place" does He have? Does it not say, "The heavens are My throne" [Isaiah 66:1], and "What is the place of My rest?" Where is there a place that we can say, "Behold, **the Lord** goes out from His place"? Is He not called "The Place of the World"? Is He not called "Blessed be the Place"? Our Sages of blessed memory said: "He is the place of the world, but the world is not His place" <note>This famous Midrashic teaching explains that God encompasses the universe, but the universe does not encompass God.</note>. Rather, the explanation of the verse is this: Behold, **the Lord**, which is the secret of the Name that includes both the Attributes of Mercy and Judgment, "goes out from His place"—meaning the place of Mercy—and comes to the Attribute of Judgment, clothing Himself in the attribute called *Elohim* <note>The Name *Elohim* is traditionally associated with strict justice and the natural order.</note>. For the primary "dwelling" of **the Lord**, may He be blessed, is always within the Attribute of Mercy. This is what our Sages of blessed memory meant when they said: "A God who is a King sitting upon a throne of mercy"—He sits there always. But the Attribute of Judgment is not His primary dwelling place; He only utilizes it when it is necessary during a time of judgment.

### **Another** even more elevated explanation 
of this verse and its legendary meaning: "Behold, **the Lord** goes out from His place." Our Sages of blessed memory said: He goes out from the Attribute of Mercy and comes to the Attribute of Judgment. You already know that three Names are held in a chain from top to bottom, and they are: *Adonai*, **the Lord**, and *Adonai* <note>original: אדנ"י יה"ו אדנ"י</note>. The essence of all these is the Name *Adonai*, which is the "place" for **the Lord**.

<column-break/>

And the Name **Mighty** <note>original: אדיר (*Adir*)</note> is entirely mercy. When He "goes out" from that place of **Mighty**—which is Mercy and the dwelling place of **the Lord**, may He be blessed—and comes to the Name *Adonai* below, which is the Attribute of Judgment, He judges His creatures with it. Here you have the plain meaning of the verse in its proper context. Thus, all the Holy Names are suspended from the Name of **the Lord**, may He be blessed. 

I am now entering into the explanation of the **Thirty Epithets** <term>kinuyim<note>Descriptive titles of God, like "Merciful," "Great," or "Jealous," which act as "garments" or channels for Divine energy.</note></term> and how they are attached to the three primary Names: *El*, **the Lord**, and *Elohim*. This is the secret of the verse, "And it came to pass in the thirtieth year" [Ezekiel 1:1]; these are the secret of the thirty epithets, which are also the secret of the "thirty wings." There are ten on the right side, ten in the center, and ten on the left side. Over them are three "flags" <note>or "banners."</note>. The middle flag is **the Lord**, and the Name **the Lord** seals them all. Thus, the "thirtieth year" is found to be dependent on the Name **the Lord**. He is over them all, they are all with Him, and everything depends on Him. 

One must return and make known the secret of the order of these thirty epithets: in which specific attribute each and every epithet stands. For there are twenty-seven epithets corresponding to the twenty-seven letters <note>The 22 standard letters of the Hebrew alphabet plus the 5 "final" forms of letters used at the ends of words.</note>, and the three Names over them bring the total to thirty. It is fitting that we enter into this explanation of each one of them according to the order of their levels of governance and according to the order of the qualities of Mercy and Judgment.

->And the children of Israel shall encamp,<-
->every man by his own camp,<-
->and every man by his own standard,<-
->according to their hosts.<-
<note>Numbers 1:52. This verse is used here to symbolize how the various Divine Names and epithets are organized into structured "camps" or "ranks."</note>

--- Page 87 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of divine names and attributes. The catchword at the bottom of the previous page, "that were said," links to the beginning of this section regarding the various titles and epithets used to describe God’s interaction with the world. This page explains the hierarchy of these names and how they relate to the essential Four-Letter Name (the Tetragrammaton).</meta>

**God, may He be blessed,** is destined in the future to separate the **epithets** <term>kinnuyin<note>Titles or descriptive names of God (like "Merciful" or "Great") rather than His essential, inherent Names.</note></term> from Himself. At the time of entering the Holy Place, He will leave them outside and will no longer "wear" them. These epithets are the places where the **Princes of the Nations** <term>Sarei HaUmmot<note>In Jewish mysticism, these are angelic beings or spiritual forces that govern the seventy gentile nations of the world.</note></term> take hold. 

The **Princes of the Nations** are never entirely uprooted from existence, and for this reason, the **Holy Names** <note>original: *Shemot ha-Kodesh*. These are the seven specific names of God in Hebrew tradition that are so holy they may never be erased.</note> are never erased. However, the other **epithets** which *can* be erased—such as "Merciful" and "Gracious" and the like—are like the "armies" of the Princes of the Nations, which God will eventually erase from the world. No nation will remain that does not renounce its own false deity, as the verse says: *“And many peoples shall go and say: ‘Come, and let us go up to the mountain of the Lord, to the house of the God of Jacob; and He will teach us of His ways, and we will walk in His paths’”* [Isaiah 2:3]. And it is written: *“For then I will turn to the peoples a pure language, that they may all call upon the name of the Lord, to serve Him with one accord”* [Zephaniah 3:9]. 

Therefore, you will find that the **Holy Names**, which are the perfect designations, are never erased; but the **epithets**, which are like the likenesses of the nations, are erased.

### And now
We must inform you of the secret of these **thirty epithets** through which God, may He be blessed, conducts Himself in **Judgment** <term>Din<note>The divine attribute of strict justice and law.</note></term> with His creatures. We shall explain which way each one inclines, what the specific quality of each epithet is, or toward which of the **Holy Names** it leans. 

There is a sign for this in the verse: *“and over him the banner of the tribe of Manasseh”* [Numbers 2:20], and it is written *“and his host, and those that were numbered of them.”* In these verses, you find a "Banner," a "Tribe," and "those numbered." Similarly, **the Lord**, may He be blessed, is the Essence; the **Holy Names** are the secret of the form of the **Banners**; and the other **epithets** are the secret of their **armies**.

### And before
We enter into the explanation of this, we must inform you that all the Names of God, may He be blessed, are included within the Name **Y-H-V-H** <note>The Tetragrammaton, the essential four-letter Name of God.</note>. 

How so? **Adonai** <note>original: אדנ"י, meaning "Lord"</note> is the secret of the "point" <note>The small decorative stroke or crown at the top of a Hebrew letter.</note> of the letter *Yod*, which is the first letter of the Name. The letter *Yod* itself represents the essence of the Name *Yah* <note>original: י"ה, the first two letters of the Tetragrammaton</note>. The point of the *Yod* is the secret of *Adonai*, while the first letter **He** of the Name **Y-H-V-H** is also connected to the secret of the name *Adonai*.

The secret of the letter **Vav** in the Name is the secret of **six names**: three of them are above the midpoint of the *Vav*, and three are below its midpoint. These are:
1. **El** <note>God (Mighty)</note> — to the right of the Name, above.
2. **Elohim** <note>God (Justice)</note> — to the left of the Name, above.
3. **Y-H-V-H** — in the center, above. 

And three names from the midpoint of the *Vav* and below:
4. **Y-H-V-H Tzevaot** <note>Lord of Hosts</note> — to the right, below.
5. **Elohim Tzevaot** <note>God of Hosts</note> — to the left, below.
6. **The Lord (Y-H-V-H)** — in the center, below.

Sometimes this is also called **El Shaddai** <note>God Almighty</note>. Thus, you find six names hanging from the *Vav*. Furthermore, a seventh name hangs from the letter *Vav*, which is **Eloah** <note>God</note>. This is the secret of the verse: *“But none says: ‘Where is **Eloah** my Maker?’”* [Job 35:10] <note>The author notes that the Hebrew says "Maker" in the plural, but the name is singular.</note>. Along this path, you will also hear the phrase *“the words of the living God (*Elohim Chayim*).”*

The secret of the final letter **He** of the Name is the secret of **A-D** <note>The first two letters of *Adonai*</note>, and sometimes it is called **Shaddai**. Thus, all the Holy Names are included within the Name **Y-H-V-H**. When we mention the Name **Y-H-V-H**, it comes with all the Holy Names combined. As it says: *“And the Lord (*Y-H-V-H*) my God shall come, and all the holy ones with thee”* [Zechariah 14:5]—meaning all the Holy Names are with You. You have therefore learned that everything depends on the Name of God. However, when you mention one of the other Holy Names, it speaks only of one particular aspect of the many aspects of **Y-H-V-H**, as I have informed you.

### Now
You must know, for your investigation, which letter of the Name each Holy Name refers to—such as **El**, **Elohim**, and the like. Once you know this, you will understand that all particulars depend on the General, and all Holy Names depend on the Name **Y-H-V-H**, may He be blessed. 

If so, you have learned that when a person mentions one of the other Holy Names, you should understand that God is "clothing" Himself in a specific **garment** known by that name. From that name, you can understand which **attribute** or which garment the Lord, may He be blessed, is wearing: 
* Whether it is the garment of **El**, which is the secret of **Mercy** <term>Rakhamim</term>, and its "Banner" testifies that He is a "Merciful God." 
* Or the garment of **Judgment** <term>Din</term> and **Kingship**, whose "Banner" is **Elohim**. 
* Or the garment of **Justice** <term>Mishpat</term>, which includes both Judgment and Mercy, whose "Banner" is **Y-H-V-H** in all His attributes. And this is the secret...

It describes these names as "garments" or "banners" that God wears to manifest different attributes like Mercy or Judgment.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the Divine Names interact with the world. The previous page concluded by explaining that while God's essential names are eternal, His "epithets" (titles like Merciful or Gracious) are the interface through which He governs the nations. This page shifts to the specific movement of God between the attributes of Mercy and Judgment, and begins a detailed count of the thirty secondary titles that stem from His core identity.</meta>

...As our sages, of blessed memory, said: "Behold, **the Lord (YHVH)** <note>The text uses the scribal substitution *Yadod* (ידוד) to avoid writing the Tetragrammaton, the four-letter Name of God, directly.</note> comes out of His place" [Isaiah 26:21]. This means He emerges from the **Attribute of Mercy** <term>Midat Rachamim<note>The aspect of God characterized by compassion and forgiveness.</note></term> and moves toward the **Attribute of Justice** <term>Midat HaDin<note>The aspect of God characterized by strict law, consequence, and judgment.</note></term>. 

But what "place" could He possibly have? Does it not say of Him, "Who has meted out heaven with the span" [Isaiah 40:12], and "Where is the house that you may build for Me? And where is the place of My rest?" [Isaiah 66:1]. How then can we say that "the Lord comes out of His place"? 

Is He not called the **"Place of the World"**? As our Rabbis of blessed memory said: "He is the place of the world, but the world is not His place" <note>This famous Midrashic teaching explains that God encompasses all of existence, rather than being contained within it.</note>. Rather, the explanation of the verse is this: **The Lord (YHVH)** is the mystery of the Name that encompasses both the attributes of Mercy and Justice. He "emerges from His place"—which is the source of Mercy—and enters the Attribute of Justice, "clothing" Himself in the attribute called **Elohim** <note>In Jewish tradition, the name *YHVH* represents Mercy, while the name *Elohim* represents Judgment.</note>. 

The primary "dwelling" of the Lord, may He be blessed, is always within the Attribute of Mercy. This is what our Sages meant when they said: "A King who sits upon the Throne of Mercy... He sits there always." However, the Attribute of Justice is not His primary dwelling place; He only makes use of it when the moment requires the application of strict judgment.

**Furthermore**, another superior level explains this verse. The allegorical interpretation of "Behold, the Lord comes out of His place" is as follows: our Rabbis of blessed memory said He "emerges from the Attribute of Mercy and comes into the Attribute of Justice." You already know that three Names are linked in a chain from above to below, and they are: **Adonai — YHVH — Adonai**. 

The essence of all these is the Name **Adonai**, which serves as the "place" for the Name **YHVH**. The [upper] Adonai is entirely Mercy. When He "emerges" from that Name Adonai (which is Mercy and the dwelling place of the Lord), He enters the Name Adonai below, which is the Attribute of Justice. It is through this that He judges His creatures. Thus, you have the plain meaning of the scripture in its proper context. 

Behold, all the Holy Names are suspended from the Name of the Lord (YHVH), may He be blessed. I am now entering into the explanation of the **Thirty Epithets** <term>Kinnuyin<note>Secondary titles or descriptive names used for God in the Bible.</note></term>. How are they attached to the three Names: **El, YHVH, and Elohim**? This is the mystery of the verse "And it came to pass in the thirtieth year" [Ezekiel 1:1]. These "thirty years" are the mystery of the thirty epithets, which are also the mystery of the **Thirty Wings** <term>Kenafim<note>A mystical reference to the 'wings' of the Chariot or the angels that support the divine presence.</note></term>. 

Ten are on the right side, ten are in the center, and ten are on the left side. Over them are three "banners." The central banner is **YHVH**, and the Name YHVH seals them all. Thus, the "thirty years" are found to be dependent on the Name YHVH; He is above them all, they are all with Him, and everything depends on Him. 

It is necessary to state once more the mystery of the order of these thirty epithets, and in which attribute each and every one of them stands. For there are twenty-seven epithets corresponding to the **twenty-seven letters** <note>The 22 letters of the Hebrew alphabet plus the 5 "final" forms of the letters *Khaf, Mem, Nun, Pe,* and *Tzadi*.</note>. When the three primary Names are added to them, they total thirty. We are now ready to enter into the explanation of each of these according to the order of their levels of authority and according to the order of the qualities of Mercy and Justice.

The Blessed Name will in the future distinguish the epithets from Himself when He enters the Holy, and He will leave them outside and no longer wear them. These are the places where the "princes of the nations" <note>In Jewish mystical thought, every nation has a celestial representative or "prince."</note> take hold, and these princes of the nations are never entirely uprooted from the whole. Therefore, the **Holy Names** <term>Shemot HaKodesh<note>Primary names of God like YHVH or Elohim that cannot be erased according to Jewish law.</note></term> are never erased, but the other **Epithets** <term>Kinnuyin<note>Descriptive titles like "The Merciful" or "The Gracious."</note></term> which can be erased—such as "Merciful," "Gracious," and the like—are like the legions of the princes of the nations, which He will eventually erase from the world. No nation will remain that does not renounce its own god, as it says: "And many nations shall go and say, 'Come, let us go up to the **Lord (YHVH)** and to the house of the **God (Elohim)** of Jacob, and He will teach us of His ways, and we will walk in His paths'" [Isaiah 2:3]. And it is written: "For then I will turn to the peoples a pure language, that they may all call upon the name of the **Lord (YHVH)**, to serve Him with one accord" [Zephaniah 3:9]. Therefore, you will find that the Holy Names which are synonyms for the Name are not erased, but the Epithets which are like the nations are erased.

### And Now
->We must inform you of the secret of these<-
->thirty epithets through which<-
the Blessed Name conducts Himself in judgment with His creatures. Each and every one is explained according to which side it leans, the specific quality of each epithet, and which of the Holy Names it inclines toward. The sign for this is: "And over him shall be the tribe of Manasseh; and his host, and those who were numbered of them" [Numbers 2:20]. You will find a "banner," you will find a "tribe," and you will find "those who were numbered." ### And Before
->we enter into explaining this, we must<-
->inform you that all the names of<-
the Blessed Name are included in the name **Lord (YHVH)**, blessed be He. How so? **Lord (Adonai)** is the secret of the "tip of the Yod" <note>The small stroke at the top of the first letter of the Name YHVH.</note> which is the first letter of the Name. The secret of **Yah (YH)** is the secret of the substance of the *Yod* itself, for the letter *Yod* is the secret of *Yah*. The tip of the *Yod* is the secret of **Adonai**, and the secret of the [first] letter **Hey** <note>The second letter of YHVH.</note> is the secret of the **Lord (YHVH)** as it appears in the epithet **God (Elohim)**—this is the secret of the first letter *Hey* within the name of the Blessed Lord. The secret of the letter **Vav** <note>The third letter of YHVH, which has a numerical value of six.</note> of the Name is the secret of six names:

three are above its midpoint and three are below its midpoint. These are: **God (El)** to the right of the *Vav* and above; **God (Elohim)** to the left of the *Vav* and above; and the **Lord (YHVH)** in the middle of the *Vav* and above. Then three from the middle of the *Vav* and below: **Lord of Hosts (YHVH Tzvaot)** to the right, **God of Hosts (Elohim Tzvaot)** to the left, and the **Lord (YHVH)** in the middle of the lower half of the *Vav*. Sometimes this is called **God Almighty (El Shaddai)**. Thus, we find six names hanging from it. Furthermore, a seventh name depends on it like the letter *Vav* itself, which is **Eloah** <note>original: אלוה</note>. This is the secret of the verse: "But none says, 'Where is **God (Eloah)** my Maker [Makers]?'" [Job 35:10]—it does not say "my Maker" in the singular but "Makers" <note>The Hebrew grammar here suggests a plural form, hinting at the multiple names/attributes.</note>. And it says, "**God (Eloah)** comes from Teman" [Habakkuk 3:3]. In this way, you will also hear the words **Living God (Elohim Chayim)**. The secret of the final **Hey** of the Name is the secret of **Adonai**, and sometimes it is called **Shaddai**. Thus, all Holy Names are included in the name of the Blessed **Lord (YHVH)**. When we mention the name **Lord (YHVH)**, it comes with all the Holy Names, as in: "And the **Lord (YHVH)** my **God (Elohim)** shall come, and all the holy ones with thee" [Zechariah 14:5]—meaning all the Holy Names are with You. You have therefore learned that everything depends on the Name of the Lord. But when you mention one of the other Holy Names, it speaks only of one specific detail from the details of **Lord (YHVH)**, as I have informed you.

### Now
->you must know to contemplate<-
->to which letter of the letters of the Name<-
that Holy Name alludes—such as **God (El)**, **God (Elohim)**, and the like. After you know this, you will understand that all details depend on the general rule, and all Holy Names depend on the name of the Blessed **Lord (YHVH)**. If so, you will find that when one mentions one of the other Holy Names, you should understand that the Lord is "clothing" Himself in a known "garment" <note>The "garment" is a common Kabbalistic metaphor for how God limits His infinite light to interact with the world through a specific attribute.</note>. From that name, you can understand which attribute or which garment the Blessed **Lord (YHVH)** is wearing: either the garment of **God (El)**, which is the secret of **Mercy (Rachamim)** and its banner testifies that He is a "Merciful God"; or the garment of **Judgment (Din)** and **Kingship (Malchut)**, whose banner is **God (Elohim)**; or the garment of **Justice (Mishpat)**, which includes both Judgment and Mercy, and its banner is the **Lord (YHVH)** in all His attributes. This is the secret of what we have said.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Divine Names, specifically how the four physical elements (Fire, Water, Air, Earth) mirror the structure of the celestial realms. It uses the page number 34 (לד) and transitions from the general nature of the elements to the specific attributes of the Name "El" (God) and "YHVH" (The Lord/Tetragrammaton). The scribe uses "Yadod" (יד"וד) as a substitute for the Tetragrammaton to maintain the sanctity of the Name.</meta>

# And After
you have understood this, I must inform you of a great principle upon which one must rely, and through which you may enter into the **Higher Secrets** <term>Sodot Elyonim<note>Deep mystical insights into the nature of God and the universe.</note></term>. You should know that the Blessed Name created water. Now, man receives many kinds of benefits from water, and even though a person touches water directly, they are not harmed by that touch. However, if a person touches fire itself, their very body is burned, as the verse says: "Can a man take fire in his bosom, and his clothes not be burned?" [Proverbs 6:27]. If this is so, then these two elements—Fire and Water—are of great necessity to the world. For if there were no fire, nothing would be warmed and there would be no life in the world; and if there were no water, everything would be consumed by fire. What did the Holy One, Blessed be He, do? He took a portion of the fire and a portion of the water and from them He made **Spirit** <note>Hebrew: *Ruach*, which can mean wind, air, or spirit.</note>. He placed the Spirit as a mediator between the water and the fire. Thus, the Spirit is found to be the intermediary between cold and heat—cold from the water and heat from the fire. It is therefore the middle ground. In this same manner, all the other "links of the **Chariot**" <term>Merkavah<note>A reference to the 'Divine Chariot' in Ezekiel's vision, used in Kabbalah to describe the structure of the spiritual dimensions.</note></term> are arranged: the Earth is between the Water and the Fire, the Fire is between the Earth and the Spirit, and the Water is between the Spirit and the Earth. Thus, the four elements circulate through one another by means of intermediaries, and in this way, they are sustained. Just as the elements are structured below, so is the way of the elements above. The **Emanated Beings** <term>Atzilim<note>Refers to the Sefirot or divine emanations in the world of Atzilut (Emanation).</note></term> from the Supreme Radiance—if there were no intermediary between them, they would never be linked, and everything would exist in a state of separation. You will find no "Simple Attribute" in its absolute totality except from the "Tip of the Yod" <note>The uppermost point of the first letter of God's name, representing the highest, most unknowable level of divinity.</note> and above. His intermediate attributes, such as "Merciful" and "Gracious" and their likes, are drawn from known sources, and their action corresponds to the source from which they are drawn. I shall now explain this to you with the help of the Name:

### The Name
->that stands on the right side is<-
->**El** and His banner is engraved as **EL'**<-
And He rules over the water, and His sign is "The God of Glory <unclear>mark</unclear>

---

thundered, the **Lord (YHVH)** <note>Original: *Yadod* (יד"וד)</note> upon many waters" [Psalm 29:3]. His epithets are founded upon the foundation of the **Higher Waters**. Do not let it enter your mind that these are physical waters; rather, the Higher Waters are "Living Waters." Our father Abraham, peace be upon him, dug wells of water and established the attribute of **Loving-kindness** <term>Chesed<note>The Sefirah of expansive benevolence, often associated with the patriarch Abraham and the element of water.</note></term> within the attribute of *El*. These epithets belong to the attribute of *El*, which consists of nine. And these are they: "**Great**" (*Gadol*)—this is the Name from the attribute of Loving-kindness. "Great" is the Name that comes from the Source, and it is the attribute known to the Kabbalists called "**Water within Water**." This means that when you examine this attribute, you will find mercy on every side, whether from the front or from the back. Therefore, wherever you see the word "Great," it refers to the head of the attribute of Loving-kindness, which is the secret of the clear and pure waters at the top of the barrel. There are two sides to the attribute of "Great": the side facing upward is called "**Merciful**" (*Rachum*), and the side facing downward is called "**Gracious**" (*Channun*). Even though "Merciful" and "Gracious" are both of the attribute of "Water within Water," the water at the top of the barrel is not equal to the water beneath it. The attribute of "Water-Merciful" refers to the clear, pure waters at the very top of the barrel, and the name "Merciful" is given to the waters adjacent to them. The attribute of "Gracious" is that portion of the water which is adjacent to the Spirit. In this way, you should evaluate the order of the composition of the Names and the Epithets. You will find that they are woven according to the order of the **Supreme Chariot**. From here you may enter the secret of what they said: "With the measure a man measures, so they measure out to him" [Mishnah Sotah 1:7]. Behold, I have explained this gate to you, including many "good virtues to the Omnipresent" <note>A play on a phrase from the Passover Haggadah.</note>; now, open your eyes to see how many hidden things and how many mysteries are concealed in the Torah. May the Lord teach us wonders from His Torah to perceive the secret of the Unity of His faith.

### And from
the keys, you will need to understand that the Name **YHVH** <note>Original: *Yadod* (יד"וד)</note>, blessed be He, is called
### Beauty (Tiferet)
And the reason is that this Name "clothes" itself in all the garments necessary for the world: sometimes the attribute of "**Mighty**" (*Adir*) when He has compassion on His creatures, and sometimes **Gates of Righteousness**. <note>The text ends with a sequence of numbers 9 11 2 9, possibly a reference to a specific verse or a scribal code.</note>

It highlights how Divine Mercy is mediated through different spiritual layers, much like air mediates between fire and water.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the exploration of the divine attributes, moving from the physical elements of Fire, Water, and Air to their spiritual counterparts. It maps the Divine Names—Elohim, YHVH, and El—onto a structural system of Left, Middle, and Right columns, corresponding to the "wings" of the Divine Chariot. This builds upon the previous page’s discussion of the "Spirit" (Ruach) acting as an intermediary between the opposites of Fire and Water.</meta>

## ->Left: ELOHIM<- | ->Middle: YHVH<- | ->Right: EL<-

<margin>These are its wings</margin>
<margin>These are its wings</margin>

| Left: ELOHIM <note>The Name associated with Judgment (Din).</note> | Middle: YHVH <note>The Tetragrammaton, associated here with Mercy (Rachamim).</note> | Right: EL <note>The Name associated with Lovingkindness (Chesed).</note> |
|:---|:---|:---|
| Mighty | Awesome | Great |
| Judge | Bearer of iniquity | Merciful |
| Arbiter | Overlooker of transgression | Gracious |
| Strong | Truth | Powerful |
| Mighty in strength | Supreme | Patient |
| Man of war | Exalted | Abundant in kindness |
| Heroic | Preserver of kindness | Holy |
| Visitor of iniquity | Dweller in eternity | Pious |
| One who recompenses | Holy | Forgiving |
