# The Name
The one standing on the right side, **EL**, is called God; it is the attribute of **Lovingkindness** <term>Chesed<note>The Sephirah or divine attribute representing unconditional benevolence and expansion.</note></term>, and it is the attribute of Abraham our father, peace be upon him. This is what the scripture means when it says: *“And he called there upon the name of YHVH, the Everlasting God”* [Genesis 21:33]. This is the secret of why we say: *"YHVH is the Great God."* The attribute of Lovingkindness is contained within water, which is a manifestation of power, and this is the secret of the verse: *“Who lays the beams of His upper chambers in the waters”* [Psalms 104:3]—literally His "upper chambers." This is certainly the meaning of: *“But you have excelled them all”* [Proverbs 31:29], the secret of the **Most High God** <note>original: *El Elyon*</note>, and *“Blessed be the Most High God, who has delivered your enemies into your hand”* [Genesis 14:20].

I must awaken your mind to a great principle and tell you that among all the attributes of the Holy Names, there is no single attribute that does not intermingle with its companion. For instance: from the level of **God** <note>original: *Elohim*</note> and below, you will find no attribute of Mercy that does not contain a bit of the attribute of Judgment, and you will find no attribute of Judgment that does not contain Mercy. If you should ask: "If so, why do you call it the attribute of Judgment?"

**Know** that the attribute whose essence and majority is Mercy, even though it contains a small amount of Judgment, is called the "Attribute of Mercy." Conversely, any attribute whose essence and majority is Judgment, even though it contains a small amount of Mercy, we call the "Attribute of Judgment."

You should know that this is the case, for if you do not say so, one would be found **"cutting the shoots"** <term>Kotzetz ba-Neti'ot<note>A classic Jewish metaphor for heresy, specifically the error of separating the divine attributes and failing to recognize the unity of God.</note></term> and causing separation in the **Unity** <term>Yichud<note>The essential oneness of God and the harmonious integration of all divine powers.</note></term>. For if you were to say that the attribute of Mercy is entirely separated from the attribute of Judgment, then they would never be able to join together; and if they did join, the Unity would be fractured. 

And if you should ask: "Behold, the attribute of Mercy is the opposite of the attribute of Judgment—how then can two opposites join as one?" Know that my words concern the secret of **Emanation** <term>Atzilut<note>The highest of the four spiritual worlds in Kabbalah, where the divine light is most pure and the Sefirot emerge.</note></term> and the binding of the **Spheres** <term>Sefirot<note>The ten creative powers or attributes through which the Infinite reveals itself.</note></term>, and I shall now enlighten your eyes:

**Know** that the attribute of Lovingkindness begins as "pure flour"—pure kindness. But as it spreads out, it proceeds to become "thickened." This thickness is the likeness of Judgment. In that thickness, which contains a bit of Judgment, the attribute of Judgment attaches itself. Furthermore, the attribute of Judgment is not entirely "pure judgment" at the point where it attaches, because it mingles with the attribute of Lovingkindness at that junction. Only when the attribute of Judgment moves further away toward the furthest extreme does it become "Strong Judgment." Thus, we find the two extremes of Lovingkindness and Judgment are strong in their respective natures, while the middle ground is comprised of both of them, and is not as intense in one specific nature as the two extremes are. This is the secret of the existing Emanations and the binding of the **Chariots** <term>Merkavot<note>The "Chariots" or spiritual structures that carry and manifest the divine will in the lower worlds.</note></term>.

<summary>This page explains the Kabbalistic structure of the Divine Names, mapping them into columns of Lovingkindness (Right), Judgment (Left), and Mercy (Middle). It emphasizes the "Secret of Emanation," where divine attributes are not isolated but intermingled to maintain the unity of the Godhead.</summary>
<keywords>Chesed, Din, Rachamim, Sefirot, Atzilut, YHVH, Elohim, El, Unity, Emanation, Divine Chariot, Divine Names</keywords>

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>This page continues the analysis of the Divine Attributes by moving into the Middle Column of the Sephirotic system. Having previously established the "wings" of Lovingkindness (Right) and Judgment (Left), the text now explores the mediating qualities of Knowledge (Da'at), Truth (Emet), and Mercy (Rachamim). These attributes are presented as the "Seal" or "Balance" that allows the world to exist by tempering strict judgment with compassion.</meta>

# The Attribute of Knowledge
Between the two Names, "The Lord is a God of Knowledge" <note>original: "El De’ot YHVH" from 1 Samuel 2:3</note>, everything is hinted at in this place. Therefore, at the beginning of the requests [in the Standing Prayer], He established: "You graciously grant man **knowledge**." And it is written: *"Because you have rejected knowledge, I will also reject you from being a priest to m<unclear>e</unclear>; seeing you have forgotten the law of your God, I will also forget your children"* [Hosea 4:6]. For this reason, the Kingdom <term>Malchut<note>The tenth and final Sephirah, representing the physical world and the feminine aspect of the Divine.</note></term> is called the **Tree of Knowledge**—meaning it is a branch of Knowledge, just as you might say "the house of so-and-so."

All of this is explained: Therefore, even "without knowledge, the soul is not good" [Proverbs 19:2], because "Good" is the tip of the Lower Knowledge, which is the "Mighty One of Jacob." Therefore, **Knowledge** stands in the middle. The Sages said: "A person's knowledge should always be integrated with other people" <note>A Talmudic idiom suggesting that spiritual knowledge must be balanced with social empathy and human connection.</note>. This is the Sephirah called:

## ->**Truth**<-

I shall now explain. First, He is called **Merciful**, then **Gracious**, then **Long-suffering**, and then **Abundant in Kindness** <note>These are the "Thirteen Attributes of Mercy" from Exodus 34:6-7.</note>. If all these ten attributes are not enough, then comes **Truth**. 

**Merciful** is at the beginning of creation, as in: *"When it burst forth, as if it had issued out of the womb"* [Job 38:8]. Afterward, He is **Gracious**, for the Gracious one gives a free gift from the "womb" and from His own. After that, man sinned, and He is **Long-suffering**, hoping man might return in repentance. The reason for this is that the weapons of Judgment stand at a distance; they *"come from a far country, from the end of heaven"* [Isaiah 13:5]. However, God is "near to all who call upon Him" [Psalms 145:18], resting His glory in our land. 

If a person merits all this, it is well; but if not, his iniquities are weighed. He "passes over the first, then the next" <note>A reference to the Divine practice of forgiving initial sins to encourage repentance.</note>, and **Abundant in Kindness** tips the scales toward kindness. But if a person is still not worthy, the law "pierces the mountain" <note>A legal idiom meaning that strict justice must be carried out regardless of the circumstances.</note>, and this is the secret of **Truth** and the final verdict. 

One who understands these secrets will understand how great the mercy of the Blessed Name is upon His creatures, and how He judges them with compassion, though in the end, there is **Justice**. This is the secret of the verse: *"And You, O Lord, are a God full of compassion, and gracious, long-suffering, and abundant in mercy and truth"* [Psalms 86:15]. 

How many great, hidden treasures are sealed in the Torah! Woe to the people for the humiliation of the Torah! Concerning this secret, the Sages enacted the blessing: "Blessed be the True Judge," for His judgment is **Truth**. "Execute the judgment of truth... and show mercy and compassion every man to his brother" [Zechariah 7:9]. 

This is because the attribute of **Truth** is the secret of the **Middle Line**. You have *El* (God) on the right side and *Elohim* (God) on the left side, with *YHVH* in the middle, which is Truth. The word for Truth <note>original: "Emet"</note> consists of the letters Aleph (the first), Mem (the middle), and Tav (the last), representing the beginning, middle, and end of the alphabet. As it is written: *"The Lord God is Truth"* [Jeremiah 10:10], and it is written: *"You give truth to Jacob"* [Micah 7:20]. All is explained in these places.

# The Sephirah called:
## ->**Mercy**<-

I shall now explain for those who know the secret of the Chariot <term>Merkavah<note>The "Chariot" refers to the earliest form of Jewish mysticism, focusing on the visionary experience of the Divine Throne.</note></term> and the secret of Inner Mercy. The verse *"And through knowledge shall the chambers be filled"* [Proverbs 24:4] allows one to understand the secret of *"I do earnestly remember him still; therefore my bowels yearn for him; I will surely have mercy upon him, says the Lord"* [Jeremiah 31:20]. 

One who understands the secret of the **Womb** <note>In Hebrew, the word for Mercy (Rachamim) shares the same root as the word for Womb (Rechem).</note> will understand the secret of *"Who will show me grace as in the months of old"* [Job 29:2] and the secret of the **mother's womb**. So many hidden treasures are in the phrase *"When it burst forth, as if it had issued out of the womb"* [Job 38:8]. If the world were not sustained by Mercy, the child formed in the mother's womb would not survive. Everything is arranged perfectly.

However, from the perspective of the Supreme Crown <term>Keter<note>The highest of the Sephirot, representing Divine Will.</note></term>, it is written: *"I will be gracious to whom I will be gracious"* [Exodus 33:19]—even if they do not deserve free gifts—and *"I will show mercy on whom I will show mercy."* This is the secret of the exit from the woman's womb. After emerging from the womb, He returns to do good, placing **Merciful** before **Gracious**: *"The Lord, the Lord God, Merciful and Gracious."* Thus, "Gracious" surrounds "Merciful" before and after creation. 

Happy are those who enter the chambers of Wisdom and merit the secrets of the Torah! Therefore it is written: *"The sounding of your bowels and of your mercies toward me, are they restrained?"* [Isaiah 63:15]. Open your eyes and see the hidden wonders in the Torah!

<margin>
Because the attribute of Mercy is such, it gave birth to two. From the "commotion of the bowels" <note>A poetic reference to deep compassion.</note>, He entered and opened the locked doors.
</margin>

**And Know** that this attribute is bound to **Jacob** and the **Ten Tribes** in the world of Mercy. *"And the Lord saw that Leah was hated, and He opened her womb"* [Genesis 29:31]. So it is with the other barren women mentioned in Scripture where it says "opening the womb." Therefore, the herald cries: "In mercy, remember!" For the "Remembrances" <note>A reference to the Zichronot (Remembrance) section of the Rosh Hashanah liturgy.</note> are adjacent to Mercy, as in: *"I will surely remember him still... [I will have] mercy."*

<summary>This page discusses the Middle Column of the Sephirot, specifically focusing on how Knowledge (Da'at) and Truth (Emet) balance Divine Mercy and Judgment. It highlights the linguistic and mystical connection between the Hebrew word for "Mercy" and the "Womb," emphasizing that the world and life itself are sustained through this maternal-divine compassion.</summary>

<keywords>Da'at, Emet, Rachamim, Jacob, Thirteen Attributes of Mercy, Middle Line, Malchut, Merkavah, Womb, Divine Names, Torah Secrets</keywords>

--- Page 93 ---
<meta>This page continues the exploration of the Middle Column of the Sephirotic tree, focusing on the attribute of Tiferet (Beauty/Harmony) and its role as the mediator between the right side (Mercy) and the left side (Judgment). It establishes how the Written Torah and the Oral Torah correspond to different Divine levels.</meta>

This is an illustration of the attribute of God <note>original: "Elohim," the name associated with Divine Judgment</note> when He judges all creatures; the same applies to all other Names. This is the secret of **Beauty** <term>Tiferet<note>The sixth Sephirah, representing harmony, balance, and the heart of the Divine system.</note></term>. The great principle is: *"And you shall make holy garments for Aaron your brother, for honor and for beauty"* [Exodus 28:2]. As a bridegroom acts as a priest in glory <note>original: "pe'er," a wordplay on Tiferet</note>. 

This is a great secret: these "branches" <note>original: "paurot"</note> of which it is said, *"and it sent out its branches"* [Ezekiel 17:6]. Behold, the name **Beauty** ascends until the **Supreme Crown** <term>Keter<note>The highest Sephirah, representing the Divine Will.</note></term> and descends upon the **Kingdom** <term>Malchut<note>The final Sephirah, representing the physical world and the manifest presence of God.</note></term>, and it is joined on all sides. Therefore it is written: *"Yours, O Lord, is the Greatness, the Might, the Beauty, the Victory, and the Majesty"* [1 Chronicles 29:11]. For this reason, it is called the **Middle Line**.

We have already explained its significance above regarding the secret of the letter *Vav* (ו) of the Unique Name <note>The Tetragrammaton, Y-H-V-H. The letter Vav has the numerical value of six, representing the six Sephirot centered around Tiferet.</note>, which is comprised of six names. This *Vav* is called **Justice** <note>original: "Mishpat"</note>. The secret of this is: *"You have executed justice and righteousness in Jacob"* [Psalms 99:4]. There is no "Justice" that does not contain **Mercy**, for He judges His world with righteousness. "Let the law pierce the mountain" <note>A Talmudic legal maxim suggesting that strict law is unyielding; the author argues that Divine judgment is more tempered than human law.</note>. Therefore, He commanded the Great Sanhedrin <note>The supreme religious assembly in ancient Israel.</note> that they should not show [personal] mercy in judgment, but rather said: *"And they shall judge the people with righteous judgment"* [Deuteronomy 16:18]. 

However, regarding the Holy One, blessed be He: *"But the Lord of hosts is exalted in judgment, and the Holy God is sanctified in righteousness"* [Isaiah 5:16]. Deep secrets are hinted at in this verse for those who know the hidden things of the Torah. King David, peace be upon him, said before he sinned: *"As for me, I will behold Your face in righteousness"* [Psalms 17:15]. But after he sinned, he said: *"And enter not into judgment with Your servant"* [Psalms 143:2].

## **And Know**

That were it not for Justice being joined to Righteousness, creatures would have no existence through the law of Charity <note>original: "Tzedakah," which means both righteousness and charity/social justice.</note>. Therefore it is said: *"Righteousness and justice are the foundation of Your throne; kindness and truth go before Your face"* [Psalms 89:15].

## **And Know**

That "Righteousness and Justice" is the secret of **Kingdom** and **Beauty**. Kingdom is Judgment; Beauty is Judgment tempered with Mercy. This Sephirah is called the **Written Torah**, and Moses our Teacher, peace be upon him, received it, for there is no way for any other creature to receive it—except for Moses, who entered the innermost Sanctuary of the Tent and merited to serve within, as it is said: *"And the Lord spoke to Moses face to face"* [Exodus 33:11]. 

The **Oral Torah** is the Sephirah of **Kingdom** <note>Malchut</note>. Therefore, one does not write down the Oral Torah <note>Originally, the Talmud and Midrash were not to be written; the author explains this through a mystical lens of maintaining the distinction between the "Male" (Written) and "Female" (Oral) aspects of divinity.</note>, so as not to establish for it a place apart from the Written Torah. This is because *"a slanderer separates a prince"* <note>Proverbs 16:28; here interpreted as separating the Divine attributes.</note>. For the Oral Torah has no existence except through the "kiss" of the Written Torah. This is the true and complete Unity. Behold, the Tablets upon which the Torah was given were in the Ark; happy is the people for whom this is so, for in their hands is the perfect Unity, and the Unique Name dwells among them. This is the Sephirah called in the Torah:

## **You** <note>Hebrew: "Attah"</note>

And the sign is: *"You, O Lord, are awesome"* <note>A paraphrase of Psalms 76:8</note>. Behold, the Sephirah of the **Crown** is called:

## **He** <note>Hebrew: "Hu"</note>

Because it cannot be grasped at all. But this **Middle Line** is called **"You"** because the Prophets perceive it by means of the **Kingdom**. And the attribute of Kingdom is called:

## **I** <note>Hebrew: "Ani"</note>

Because it is perceived by those who possess the **Holy Spirit**. These matters are among the "hidden things of the world." Let him who has eyes see. This Sephirah is also called:

## **Awesome** <note>Hebrew: "Nora"</note>

And the sign is: *"Awesome in praises, doing wonders"* [Exodus 15:11]. This is the attribute of Jacob. When he attached himself to it, it is written: *"And he was afraid, and said: How awesome is this place!"* [Genesis 28:17]. This is the secret of *"The Great, the Mighty, and the Awesome"* <note>The opening of the Amidah prayer.</note>—corresponding to Abraham (Greatness), Isaac (Might), and Jacob (Beauty/Awesome). It is called "Awesome" because through it all existing things and all "mirrors" <note>original: "aspaklariyot," a term for prophetic visions or lenses</note> see. I am hinting to you: *"I will praise You, for I am fearfully and wonderfully made"* [Psalms 139:14]. This is the secret of the connection of the Sephirot in the term "Awesome." 

This Sephirah is also called:

## **Knowledge** <note>Hebrew: "Da'at"</note>

I shall now explain the connection of the Upper Sephirot: **Wisdom** (Chokhmah), **Understanding** (Binah), and **Crown** (Keter). Behold, **Knowledge** is the secret of the **Middle Line** that takes hold of both the Crown and the Kingdom, and everything is founded upon it. This is the secret of: *"By wisdom, by understanding, and by knowledge"* [Exodus 31:3]. I shall explain the verse: *"And by knowledge shall the chambers be filled with all precious and pleasant riches"* [Proverbs 24:4]. And it is written: *"By His knowledge the depths were broken up, and the skies drop down the dew"* [Proverbs 3:20]. And it is written: *"Talk no more so exceeding proudly... for the Lord is a God of knowledge [De'ot]"* [1 Samuel 2:3]. 

He who knows the secret of **Knowledge**, which is the secret of the Middle Line, understands the secret of what our Sages of blessed memory said: "Great is Knowledge, for it was placed between... <note>The text cuts off, but refers to the Talmudic statement that Knowledge is placed between two Divine Names.</note>

<summary>This page explains Tiferet (Beauty) as the "Middle Line" that balances Divine Mercy and Judgment, linking it to the Written Torah and the patriarch Jacob. It also identifies the mystical meanings of the pronouns "He," "You," and "I" as they relate to the Sephirot of Crown, Beauty, and Kingdom.</summary>
<keywords>Tiferet, Malchut, Da'at, Middle Line, Written Torah, Oral Torah, Jacob, Moses, Divine Names, Sephirot</keywords>

--- Page 94 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of Divine Names and their role in the structure of reality. Having established the "Middle Column" (Tiferet) on the previous page, the author now explains how the Act of Creation (Ma'aseh Bereshit) and the Great Hallel (Psalm 136) reflect the balance between natural law (Judgment) and Divine sovereignty.</meta>

The Work of Creation <note>original: "Ma'aseh Bereshit"</note> was performed through the name *Elohim* <note>The Divine name associated with strict justice, law, and the boundaries of nature</note> to indicate that everything was made according to the Law. Therefore, it is not necessary to change any of the natural laws of the world to perfect it. However, it is necessary to change nature at times to demonstrate His kingdom and His dominion over all created beings, to make it known that He made everything and everything is in His hand.

## And Therefore
You will find in the psalm of the "Great Hallel" <note>Psalm 136, a central prayer of praise in Jewish liturgy</note> three names: the Tetragrammaton <note>The four-letter name Y-H-V-H</note>, *Elohim*, and *Adonai* <note>My Lord</note>. As it is said: *"Give thanks to the Lord [YHVH], for He is good... Give thanks to the God [Elohim] of gods... Give thanks to the Lord [Adonai] of lords"* [Psalm 136:1-3]. Truly, the first Name represents the essence of governance and the existence of the Blessed Ancient Name, whose thoughts are deep in secret, as it says: *"To Him who alone does great wonders"* [Psalm 136:4]. Then he returns to the second name, *Give thanks to the God of gods*, and explains in the psalm: *"To Him who by wisdom made the heavens... to Him who spread out the earth above the waters... to Him who made great lights... the sun to rule by day... the moon and stars to rule by night"* [Psalm 136:5-9].

Then he returns to the third name, which is *Adonai*, and explains through it the secret of Lordship, dominion, and governance over all created beings, as it is written: *"Whatever the Lord pleases He does, in heaven and in earth"* [Psalm 135:6]. And it says <note>original: Daniel 4:32, in Aramaic</note>: *"And according to His will He acts among the host of heaven, and there is none who can stay His hand or say to Him, 'What have You done?'"* Corresponding to this name *Adonai*, he returns to complete the third section of the psalm: *"To Him who struck Egypt in their firstborn... and brought Israel out from among them... with a strong hand and an outstretched arm... to Him who divided the Red Sea into parts... and made Israel pass through the midst of it... but overthrew Pharaoh and his army in the Red Sea"* [Psalm 136:10-15], and the rest of the psalm's completion.

Therefore, it is called the "Great Hallel" because it includes the Blessed Ancient Name in the verse *"Give thanks to the Lord [YHVH] for He is good,"* and it includes the name *Elohim* through which the world was created according to the straight Law <note>original: "Din," meaning justice or natural order</note>—where nothing needs addition or subtraction from the form prepared by the Blessed Name—and this is what is meant by *"to the God of gods."* Finally, it includes the name *Adonai*, which is the name indicating dominion and Lordship over all creations, and it is in His hand to change nature and do with it as He desires and wills, to make known to all that He is their Creator. Therefore, the name of the psalm is the "Great Hallel," the head of all types of praises. You will find in it 36 <note>original: "Lamed-Vav" (36). While Psalm 136 has 26 verses, this likely refers to a specific count of divine attributes or word-groupings in the author's tradition</note> times the word "finished" <note>original: "kalah"</note> corresponding to the Name of the Blessed One. We have already explained in the Fifth Gate that between the Divine Will and the name *Elohim* stands the Great Sanhedrin <note>The supreme council; here used as a metaphor for the Divine assembly or the source of cosmic law</note>, and there the judgment is cut according to the Truth.

## And Know
That the entire Work of Creation and the measure of the **Divine Emanations** <term>Sephirot<note>The ten attributes or channels through which God reveals Himself and creates the world.</note></term> are sealed with the name *Elohim* from beginning to end. Regarding the foundation of the initial combination of letters in the creation of the world and the "Middle Point" upon which every combination of letters depends—as recounted in the **Book of Formation** <term>Sefer Yetzirah<note>One of the earliest and most influential mystical texts on the creation of the universe through the Hebrew alphabet.</note></term>—it is the secret of *El Ya* <note>God [is] the Lord</note>. Therefore, Abraham our father (peace be upon him) informed all the inhabitants of the world and *"called there on the name of the Lord, the God of the world"* [Genesis 21:33]. This is the beginning of all the descriptive titles attached to the Name of the Blessed God.

When you remember the name *El Ya*, which is the secret of the ten levels of the Sephirot within the ten utterances through which the world was created, you will find them as twelve lights, and within them all creatures are included. The secret is: *"Behold, to the Lord your God belong the heaven and the heaven of heavens, the earth and all that is in them"* [Deuteronomy 10:14]. Therefore it says: *"All the rivers run into the sea"* [Ecclesiastes 1:7]—literally "to the sea" <note>original: "el ha-yam"</note>, which is the "wing" of *Elohim*. Yet the sea is not full, because everything depends on the Name <note>Y-H-V-H</note>, which is the foundation and the root.

Therefore, it is made known in the Torah that when the name *Elohim* is stripped of its "seals"—which come from His Great Name—it remains "mute" <note>original: "elem," a wordplay on the name "Elohim" (אלהים) minus the letters of the Tetragrammaton or its spirit, leaving "Alm" (אלם), meaning mute or silent</note>; it cannot speak or do anything. This is what is meant by the verse *"Who makes one mute... is it not I, the Lord?"* [Exodus 4:11]. When the Name of the Lord stands alone, taking away the emanation, the flow, and the soul that He placed within the name *Elohim*, it remains mute. Therefore, he says: *"Give thanks to the Lord [YHVH] for He is good,"* without changing it, and then says *"Give thanks to the God [Elohim] of gods,"* doubling the speech there. Likewise, *"Give thanks to the Lord [Adonai] of lords,"* for He is not "one" except from the side of the Name of the Blessed God. For this reason, *"He who sacrifices to any god [Elohim] shall be utterly destroyed, save unto the Lord [YHVH] only"* [Exodus 22:19].

<summary>This page explores the theological significance of the "Great Hallel" (Psalm 136) and how the Divine Names YHVH, Elohim, and Adonai represent different aspects of God's relationship to nature and law. It explains that while the world operates by natural law (Elohim), God retains the sovereign power (Adonai) to perform miracles to demonstrate His dominion.</summary>

"I will surely have mercy on him," says the Lord <note>original: "Rachem arachamenu Hashem"</note>, and Abraham held fast to the attribute of 
Kindness <note>Chesed</note> of the Lord, and Jacob to the attribute of Mercy <note>Rachamim</note>—and behold, 
the scripture brings good tidings for the Lord is good to all and His mercy is over 
all His works, for were it not for mercy, no creature would 
be born into the world. And this Sephirah is called 
**Face** <note>original: "Panim"</note>, and the secret is: "You shall see my back, 
but my face shall not be seen" [Exodus 33:23]. "Others" <note>referring to the back or external manifestations</note> 
are the secret of Kingdom <note>Malkhut</note>. Therefore, "He called to Moses 
and the Lord spoke to him," and the Name, may He be blessed, did not withhold 
from Moses his request. However, He told him that it was not 
in his power to endure it—for the Name, may He be blessed, is good and does good, 
granting the request of His pious ones. Therefore He said, "and my face 
shall not be seen"—He did not say "and my face you shall not see"—
"shall not be seen" means he is unable to see them. For if He were to give 
the permission, you would be unable to see. And as for what is said, "And the Lord spoke 
to Moses face to face"—meaning without 
any interruption in between, and He was able to speak with him 
face to face. Yet in the abundance of the Radiance <note>Zohar</note>, Moses was not able 
to see the Face. And behold, a parable: a man looks 
at the face of the eye of the sun and is unable to gaze into it—
and so it is: "you cannot see my face," because 
the vision of the eyes is weak. And likewise, "face to face the Lord spoke 

with you"—He did not say "face toward face" 
but "face within face," meaning a face in the way of a face. 
And this is the meaning of "Behold, I come to you in a thick cloud"—
and it is written "out of the midst of the fire." Everything is explained, and 
we have more to add for you as an explanation at the conclusion of this 
Sephirah. It is written: "His legs are pillars of marble" <note>Song of Songs 5:15</note>—"His legs" 
refers to Victory <note>Netzach</note> and Glory <note>Hod</note>. "Pillars of marble" <note>original: "Shesh," also meaning six</note> is Beauty <note>Tiferet</note>, which is the secret 
of the six names. And after mentioning His legs, 
which are Netzach and Hod and are the pillars of six—which are 
the secret of Jachin and Boaz, the two pillars—he mentioned 
the attribute of Foundation <note>Yesod</note> and Kingdom <note>Malkhut</note>, 
and said afterward: 
"set upon bases of fine gold." One who understands the secret of these 
and their principles will enter into many "chariots" <note>mystical realms or Merkavot</note>. And upon 
this Foundation <note>Yesod</note> the Holy Temple was built with two pillars, 
and two bowls of the capitals that were on top of the pillars, 
and two networks to cover the two 
bowls of the capitals which were on top of the pillars, 
according to the orders of the Sephirot above and below. 
Furthermore, you will see in this many secrets and many treasures. 
See, I have opened before you the gates 
of the knowledge of the Name, may He be blessed—
the Unique One.

# The Name
# Elohim

The sixth of the Sephirot is <note>likely referring to the position in a specific kabbalistic system or the transition from the previous discussion</note>
and we have already 
explained 
to you in the fifth gate the matter of the levels by way 
of the Name of the Lord, may He be blessed—how He clothes Himself 
in names and titles according to the need to conduct Himself with 
the people of the world. And in that same attribute that 
He needs to conduct Himself, by that attribute He is called at that 
hour.

**And now** you must know that the attribute of 
Might <note>Gevurah</note> is called Elohim, and this is 
Absolute Judgment in which there is no change. Therefore, 
the world was created with the name Elohim to say that all 

the Works of Creation <note>Ma'aseh Bereshit</note> are made according to Truth 
and perfect Wisdom, like all the creatures according to their kinds 
among the celestial and the terrestrial. And likewise the limbs of every 
single creature among the various creatures that differ from one another—
all of them are made with the ultimate perfection and the proper 
arrangement for every single creature and every single limb. 
And this is what Solomon, peace be upon him, said: "for every 
work of God [Elohim] shall be forever; nothing 
can be added to it, and nothing taken from it" [Ecclesiastes 3:14]. And the reason is 
because the Name, may He be blessed, made them all with ultimate 
perfection; nothing in the world can be 
more perfect than the form which the Name, 
may He be blessed and exalted, prepared. Therefore, it is mentioned in every 
act.

All desires are dependent [on Him]. 
Therefore, the Name, may He be blessed, because He emanates 
and creates everything, gives without any deficiency, for 
everything was created and made by His will. 
And there is no lack at all when He gives to every seeker 
their request; and this is the secret of "Open Your hand 
and satisfy the desire of every living thing" [Psalm 145:16]. He 
lacks nothing when He gives enough for the needs of every seeker, 
since by His will everything is made. And this is a great secret: 
that the Thought through which the world was created 
is called Will <note>original: "Ratzon," referring to the primordial Divine desire to create</note>. Therefore, know that toward the 
left side—which is the opposite of the right—stand all the attributes 
of Judgment <note>original: "Din"</note>. If the created beings sin, they are judged 
by the attribute of the Left, and good is withheld from them. Therefore it is written: 
"The face of the ox was on the left" [Ezekiel 1:10], for there is the accuser <note>term: "Katigor" (from the Greek "kategoros"), a prosecuting attorney in the heavenly court</note> of all 
litigants. And on that same side, the Satan <note>The adversary or accuser</note> prosecutes 
between his horns. Therefore, this Sephirah <note>Divine emanation</note> is called:

### North

->From which are emanated the powers<-
->of Judgment and the Accuser, and this is<-

the secret of "Out of the North evil shall break forth" [Jeremiah 1:14]. Because it is 
the cause of opposites and deficiencies, it is called North <note>original: "Tzaphon"</note> for 
a reason I will tell you:

### Know

->That when all the<-
->creatures seek<-

a thing from the Crown <note>term: "Keter," the highest Sephirah</note>, which is the world of Kindness and Mercy, 
the requests of every seeker are granted. This bounty is drawn 
from above, whether the seeker is righteous or 
a sinner. But when that gift arrives—
having been requested—by way of the North (for that is the passage 
for all requests and desires), then those 
powers and groups standing on the Northern side 
contest that gift, 
demanding that the person stand in judgment over it. If that seeker is worthy 
of this request, they fulfill his desire. But if 
he is not worthy, he will not succeed in obtaining his request. 
Then they withhold the gift in that place, and it 
never returns above—because 
above they give and do not take back—yet it 
does not descend to that seeker, because he is not worthy 
of it according to the law of Judgment. Thus, that request 
and gift stands [frozen] in that place. 
Therefore it is called North <note>original: "Tzaphon," which shares a root with "Tzafun," meaning hidden or stored</note>, for there 
requests and gifts are "hidden." And this is the secret of "Out of the North 
evil shall break forth," and "Out of the North gold comes" [Job 37:22], and "The wealth of the sinner is laid up <note>Tzafun</note> for the righteous" [Proverbs 13:22]. 
"He stores up <note>Yitzpon</note> sound wisdom for the upright" [Proverbs 2:7]. And all those 
good things hidden in the North are destined to be 
for the righteous. And this is the secret of "How great is Your goodness, 
which You have hidden <note>Tzafanta</note> for those who fear You, which You have wrought for those who trust in You 
before the sons of men" [Psalm 31:20]. "Before" <note>original: "Neged," meaning opposite</note> is meant literally, because 
on the North side there are "detainers" <note>spiritual forces that delay or block the descent of blessings</note>. And one who knows this secret 
will understand what our Sages, of blessed memory, said: 
"One who wishes to become wealthy should turn North" [Talmud, Bava Batra 25b]. See how much they 
emphasized this place! One will also understand what is written 
in the Torah regarding the Tefillin <note>term: phylacteries worn during prayer</note> being on the left: 
"And it shall be for a sign upon your hand"—meaning the weak <note>original: "kehah," meaning dim or weak</note> hand, 
which is the left, which prevents good 
through Judgment from the sinner (from the expression "and he was not dim 
against them" <note>original: "lo kehah bam," meaning he did not restrain them</note>). How fitting it is for the Tefillin to be 
in that place. And were it not for 
the Binding of Isaac <note>original: "Akeidat Yitzchak"</note>, not one of the 
created beings would be able to achieve the desire of his request, 
due to the intensity of the scrutiny of Judgment in this place. 
And one who knows this will understand the secret of "And it came to pass when Isaac was old 
and his eyes were too dim <note>original: "vatekhein," the same root as weak/kehah</note> to see" [Genesis 27:1]. 
Therefore, you find a place to do good even for the wicked, 
even though they are not worthy, 
and the reason is that while they certainly scrutinize the Judgment, 
it ascended somewhat to the World of Mercy. And it is written 
that [Isaac] was "clung to Abraham," and it says of him, "And Abraham 
was old, advanced in days." And here you find how good is withheld 
from the wicked, and the good is hidden for the righteous 
in the North. "The wealth of the sinner is stored up for the righteous," and all 
the gates of Justice.

<meta>This page continues the transition from the attribute of Gevurah (Strength/Judgment) associated with the North, moving into the mystical significance of the heart (associated with the number 32) and the relationship between Mercy and Judgment as expressed through the names of God and the creation of Heaven and Earth.</meta>

# And the one who understands
these secrets will understand the secrets sealed in the psalm that concludes the Book of Praises <note>The Book of Psalms</note>, which says: "Hallelujah. Praise God in His sanctuary; praise Him in the firmament of His power" [Psalm 150:1]. This psalm indicates the permutations of the alphabet in the *Book of Creation* <note>original: "Sefer Yetzirah," one of the oldest Jewish mystical texts</note> and their rotations. It explains how the Divine Name Y-H-V-H <note>original: "יד וד" (Y-D V-D), a scribal variation used to avoid writing the Tetragrammaton directly</note>, may He be blessed, clothes Himself in the name *El* <note>God (representing Kindness)</note>, sometimes in the name *Elohim* <note>God (representing Judgment)</note>, and sometimes in *Eleh* <note>"These"</note>—half in this and half in that. Here lie deep secrets regarding the fact that you will find the name *Elohim* thirty-two times in the Story of Creation [Genesis 1], corresponding to the "Thirty-two Wondrous Paths of Wisdom" through which the world was created in the combination of *Elohim*. Therefore, among all the letters, there is no letter in which the Name clothes itself individually, except for the thirty-two letters <note>A reference to the 22 letters of the Hebrew alphabet plus the 10 Sephirot/numbers, totaling 32 paths</note>.

->**To me, to her, to him; in him, in her, in him**<-

There are great chambers dwelling in this place. Therefore, the "Heart" <note>In Hebrew, "Heart" is *Lev* (ל"ב), which has the numerical value of 32</note> sustains the spirit and is the king within the body, and the spirit sustains the soul <note>original: "Neshamah"</note> in the secret of "And He breathed into his nostrils the breath of life" [Genesis 2:7]. One who knows this secret knows the secret of the spirit’s connection to the soul in the secret of Y-H-V-H *Elohim*: "And Y-H-V-H God formed man" [Genesis 2:7]—a full Name over a full world. These matters are among the inner mysteries. You must contemplate well the order of the emanation of abundance. Y-H-V-H, may He be blessed, is above all names and titles, for they are all supported by Him, and they are like thrones for His great Name. And this is the Sephirah <note>Divine emanation</note> called:

### Left

the secret of Mercy <note>original: "Rachamim"</note> when it takes hold of the Lord <note>original: "Adonai"</note>. The reason is that He creates everything, and He is full of mercy and kindness. However, every name that arises through the renewal of the world represents the quality of Judgment <note>term: "Din," the attribute of limit, law, and justice</note>, because it is something "made" and not the "maker." Anything that is "made" has a boundary and an end, and this is the secret of absolute Judgment—that deficiency clings to created beings, and everything deficient needs to draw from and receive [from another].

***

Therefore, it is entirely Judgment. And if it sometimes influences and gives, it is only because it receives in the moment it gives; for if it does not receive, it is impossible for it to give since deficiency is attached to it. This is the secret of the connection of all the "Chariots" <note>Divine structures/assemblages</note> in the secret of "Earth and Heaven." For certainly Earth was created first, and that Earth is called "Heaven" relative to what is below it. This is the secret of what is said: "Y-H-V-H by wisdom founded the earth; by understanding He established the heavens" [Proverbs 3:19], and "His glory is above the earth and heaven" [Psalm 148:13]. The secrets of the connections of the Sephirot and the levels depend on this. 

The one who understands these deep secrets will understand the secret of the two verses that the School of Shammai and the School of Hillel <note>Two famous opposing schools of Rabbinic thought</note> debate. One side says that the heavens were created first, as it is said: "In the beginning God created the heavens and the earth" [Genesis 1:1]. The other side says the earth was created first, as it is said: "On the day that Y-H-V-H God made earth and heaven" [Genesis 2:4].

### And behold, I

am hinting at the order of the levels arranged here in the gateway of the memory of their excellence. When speaking of their excellence, it says "Heaven and Earth," but at the time of their receiving and influencing, it says "Earth and Heaven." There is no "Heaven" among all existing things that does not have an "Earth" preceding it <note>Meaning a vessel to receive the influence</note>, and there is no "Earth" that is not also "Heaven" from the perspective of the intermediate levels—except for the Supreme Crown <note>Keter</note>, may He be blessed, which is the "True Heaven," giving and not receiving; and except for the "Earth" called Kingship <note>term: "Malchut," the final Sephirah</note> among the ten Sephirot, which is called "Earth" alone, for she receives from all of them and does not influence back into them. However, the one who understands these secrets will understand what the scripture says: "The earth You founded... and the heavens are the work of Your hands" [Psalm 102:25].

### Know

that every receiver must necessarily contain the quality of Judgment. Behold, the name *Elohim* is the first title regarding the "Eternal Covenant" because the orders of Judgment were sealed there. Therefore, it is called "Left" in every place, and it is the place of Judgment. From that side—which is the beginning of the character of Creation—the [lower forces] are emanated.

It explains that "Heaven" represents the giving principle while "Earth" represents the receiving principle, reconciling ancient Rabbinic disputes about which was created first.</summary>

It is judged, and the servants must be a flaming fire <note>original: "אש לוהט" (esh lohet). This refers to Psalm 104:4, describing the angels/servants of God as fire, reflecting the attribute of Gevurah</note>—
for this is the secret of the place of immersion for those entering the King's

and this attribute is called:

### The Rock
Regarding the wonders of He who "brings forth 
water for you from the flinty rock" <note>original: "צור החלמיש" (tzur hachalamish); see Deuteronomy 8:15</note>.
We have already explained this in the word *Vayotzer* <term>And He formed (ויוצר)</term> in the beginning. Know

"Abraham begot Isaac" <note>In Kabbalah, this signifies that Kindness (Abraham) gave rise to Judgment (Isaac)</note>; and "he took in his hand the

together" <note>Genesis 22:6</note>. In short, this is linked to the Fifth Gate <note>The Sephirah of Gevurah is the fifth when counting down from the top</note>

### Wealth
Sometimes for good and sometimes 
for evil, for "wealth is kept 
for its owner to his hurt" <note>Ecclesiastes 5:12</note>. From this place comes 
red gold <note>original: "זהב אדום" (zahav adom). In mystical thought, red symbolizes the heat of Judgment</note>, which is only merited through Judgment (*Din*). From 
this attribute is awakened the quality of the *Teruah* <term>The broken, sobbing sound of the Shofar</term> 
when it battles against enemies. However, 
on Rosh Hashanah <note>The Jewish New Year</note>, Abraham and Jacob surround it with a "simple" sound <note>the *Tekiah*, a long, straight shofar blast representing Mercy</note>; 
therefore, a simple blast precedes it and a simple blast 
follows it. "Happy is the nation that knows the *Teruah*" <note>Psalm 89:16</note>, 
for they know how to use it and they know how it 
will proceed. "Can a man walk on coals 
and his feet not be scorched? 
Can a man carry coals 
in his lap and his clothes not 
be burned?" <note>Proverbs 6:28</note> Therefore, 
the wall 
of Jericho fell and was swallowed up by means of the *Teruah*. 
We must awaken it on Rosh Hashanah with the attribute of Mercy <note>original: "מדת הרחמים" (midat ha-rachamim)</note>, 
Abraham and Jacob. "God has ascended with a blast (*Teruah*), the Lord 
with the sound of a shofar" <note>Psalm 47:6</note>. Therefore, "when you blow a *Teruah* 
a second time... they shall journey" <note>Numbers 10:6</note>. And it is written, "Rise up, O Lord, and let Your enemies be scattered." 
"But when the assembly is to be gathered, you shall blow, but not sound a *Teruah*." "When 
you go to war in your land... then you shall sound an alarm (*Teruah*)." And on a day 
of joy, and your festivals, "you shall blow the trumpets." And the day of 
Rosh Hashanah, even though it is the New Year, "it shall be a day of *Teruah* for you" 
at the appointed time for our festival day, because Abraham and 
Jacob surround it. And we conclude [the prayer] with: "You hear the sound 
of the Shofar... You are from of old and You listen to the *Teruah*, and there is none 
like You, You Who hears the voice of the *Teruah* of His people Israel 
today in Mercy." Upon this judgment, they established the secret of the Laws 
bound to Gevurah <term>Strength/Judgment</term>. And this Sephirah is called:

### The Arm
For He wages war and takes 
vengeance for Israel. That is to say, 
when the Kingdom <term>Malkhut</term> dons the armor of Strength: 
"Awake, awake, put on strength, O Arm of the Lord, as in the days of old, 
the ancient generations" <note>Isaiah 51:9</note>. It is written: "By the greatness of Your arm they are as still 
as a stone" <note>Exodus 15:16</note>. And it is written: "The eternal God is a dwelling place, and underneath 
are the everlasting arms" <note>Deuteronomy 33:27</note>. That is, when both are mentioned, 
it says "arms" (plural); but when one arm is mentioned, it is 
the Left <note>The left arm represents Gevurah/Judgment, while the right is Chesed/Kindness</note>, for "Your right hand and Your arm are His glory." 
And she <note>The Shekhinah/Kingdom</note> 
clothes herself in the Arm when the Kingdom clothes itself 
in the Arm of Gevurah. He who understands this secret will understand 
what the Sages, of blessed memory, said regarding Rabbi Hanina ben Dosa 
and his companions: "The whole world is sustained 
by his merit, yet he is sustained 
by the Arm 
of God" <note>A mystical reinterpretation of a Talmudic passage (Berakhot 17b) which usually says he was sustained by a small measure of carobs; here, his sustenance is linked to the Divine Arm</note>.

<summary>This page explores the Sephirah of Gevurah (Judgment), identifying it with fire, "The Rock," "Wealth," and "The Arm." It explains the liturgical use of the Shofar, where the "broken" sound of Judgment (Teruah) is tempered by the "straight" sounds of Mercy (Tekiah).</summary>
<keywords>Gevurah, Teruah, Shofar, Rosh Hashanah, Abraham, Isaac, Jacob, The Arm, Divine Judgment, Red Gold, Kabbalistic symbolism</keywords>

--- Page 99 ---
<meta>This page continues the exploration of the Sephirah of Gevurah (Strength/Judgment). The previous page established Gevurah as the source of "The Rock" and "Wealth," linked to the red fire of judgment and the patriarch Isaac. This page further details why Isaac, representing Judgment, was drawn to Esau, and how this attribute relates to the location of animal sacrifices, the "Fear of Isaac," and the "Great Fire" through which the Torah was given.</meta>

What we have explained is the opposite of how many people mistakenly understand the plain meaning of the written text.

# And Know
Know that in this place there is a receptacle, a foothold, and success for the wicked, in order to discipline the sinners from among them. This is the secret <note>original: "וז"ס" (ve-zeh sodo). In Kabbalah, "secret" refers to the mystical or symbolic meaning behind a literal text.</note> of "And Isaac loved Esau because game was in his mouth" <note>Genesis 25:28</note>. And the secret of "Now therefore take, I pray thee, thy weapons, thy quiver and thy bow" <note>Genesis 27:3</note>. Far be it—God forbid—from Isaac to have intended to bless the wicked Esau <insert>except</insert> with the blessing appropriate for Jacob, which is the blessing of Abraham. The end of the matter proves its beginning.

One who understands the secret of the "flavors" <note>original: "טעמים" (ta'amim), meaning both the musical cantillation marks and the underlying reasons/meanings</note> that we explained regarding the word *Tzafon* <term>North / Hidden (צפון)</term> will understand the secret of the sacrifices called "Most Holy," whose slaughtering is in the north, and the receiving of their blood in a sacred vessel is in the north. "He shall slaughter it on the side of the altar northward" <note>Leviticus 1:11</note>; "The sacrifices of God are a broken spirit" <note>Psalm 51:17</note>.

Because this *Sefirah* <term>Emanation (ספירה)</term> called *Tzafon* <note>North</note> prevents the sinners from completing their desires, it is written regarding the "withering hand" <note>A reference to the weakening of the power of the "Other Side" or the wicked</note>. And this is the secret of the thief, upon whom everything depends: "He had rays coming out of his hand; and there was the hiding of his power" <note>Habakkuk 3:4</note>. "There was the hiding of his power" certainly, for it is a treasury where the hidden <insert>m</insert>atters of all the sinners are stored. Thus, this *Sefirah* is called this in every place:

# Fear
->Dominion and fear are with Him <insert>there</insert><-
"Fearers in Zion are afraid" <note>Isaiah 33:14</note>.
And this is the secret of "The Fear of Isaac" <note>Genesis 31:42</note>, and "Fear in the nights." My flesh "shudders from Your fear" <note>Psalm 119:120</note>—all of this is an allusion to this place. And this *Sefirah* is called:

# Might
->In the secret of "Yours, O Lord, is the Greatness"<-
-> "and the Might" — meaning:<-
The place of the severe, strong, and great Judgment (*Din*) where no creature can enter because of the intensity of the Judgment—except for the mighty ones who are called by its name. This is the secret of the saying: "Who is mighty? He who conquers his inclination" <note>Ethics of the Fathers 4:1</note>. This is an allusion to this place, for when a man conquers his inclination <note>original: "יצרו" (yitzro), the *Yetzer Hara* or base impulse</note>, he is then worthy to enter this palace, and all his desires shall be fulfilled. Therefore, the Sages asked: "From when do we mention the Might of the Rains?" <note>Mishnah Berakhot 5:2</note>. This means: when the world is being judged to see if they are worthy or not.

This is the secret of all the allusions in the Torah, as it is said: "I will give you the rain of your land in its due season" <note>Deuteronomy 11:14</note>, and "the treasury of the heavens." Therefore, it is judged with Might (*Gevurah*). For this reason, the "hovering mighty ones" are called so in this place, for it is the place of Fear. This is the secret of "sixty mighty ones are around it, of the mighty ones of Israel... all of them handling the sword, expert in war, because of fear in the nights" <note>Song of Songs 3:7-8</note>. This all refers to this place.

Behold, Moses our teacher—peace be upon him—ascended to this place and took the Torah with strength: "A wise man scales the city of the mighty" <note>Proverbs 21:22</note>. For the Torah was given from the Right hand by way of the Left hand. This is the secret of "And God spoke all these words" <note>Exodus 20:1</note>. The Torah was given through Might (*Gevurah*), extending from the Right to the Left hand, and was given to Moses. This is the explanation of all we have detailed above; understand this.

# And this Sephirah is called "Great Fire"
Because this fire is stronger than any fire in the world; all types of fire are like straw before it. It is the fire that licks, burns, fights, and causes judgment. Because the Torah was given through Might (*Gevurah*), it was therefore given from the midst of the fire: "from His right hand went a fiery law for them" <note>Deuteronomy 33:2</note>. And it is written: "You heard His words out of the midst of the fire" <note>Deuteronomy 4:36</note>, and "let me not see this great fire any more, that I die not" <note>Exodus 20:19</note>. Therefore, it is said that they all died and their souls departed when He spoke to them, for no creature has the power to stand before the Great Judgment which depends on Might (*Gevurah*).

From this place, all types of fire are drawn. One verse says: "He makes His angels spirits; His ministers a flaming fire" <note>Psalm 104:4</note>. The angels are "spirit" <note>or wind</note>; therefore, there is a spirit of impurity and a spirit of purity. But His ministers are "fire," as it is said: "For by fire the Lord [will contend]" <note>Isaiah 66:16</note>.

<summary>This page explores Gevurah (Might/Judgment) as the source of "Holy Fear" and the "Great Fire" of the Torah. It explains that Isaac's "Fear" and his affinity for Esau are mystical symbols of this attribute's role in disciplining sinners and governing the world through strict judgment.</summary>
<keywords>Gevurah, Might, Judgment, Fire, Isaac, Esau, Tzafon, North, Moses, Torah, Sacrifices, Fear of Isaac</keywords>

--- Page 100 ---
<meta>This page continues the exposition on the Sephirah of Gevurah (Strength/Judgment). While the previous page discussed the "North" and the hidden nature of judgment, this page explores additional titles for this attribute, such as "Rock," "Wealth," and "Arm," and details its role in the liturgy of Rosh Hashanah and the history of Israel’s battles.</meta>

<page-num>38</page-num>
->**Gates of Righteousness**<-
<sig>20 66 6 7 864</sig>
<meta>catchword: fell</meta>

...judged, and the servants <note>referring to angels or ministering spirits</note> must be a "flaming fire." This is the secret of the "place of immersion" <note>original: "מקום טבילה" (makom tevilah). A reference to the ritual bath or a spiritual purification process.</note> for those entering the Palace of Your Kindness. "And a flame scorched all the trees of the field" <note>Joel 1:19</note>. And what is this called?

## Rock
<note>original: "Tzur" (צור)</note>
Regarding the matters of wonders: "Who brought you forth water from the flinty rock" <note>Deuteronomy 8:15</note>. We have already explained this regarding the phrase "Creator of the Beginning" <note>original: "יוצר בראשית" (Yotzer Bereishit)</note>. And know that the fire emerged from the water for a residence of wonders. "Abraham fathered Isaac" <note>Genesis 25:19</note>, and "he took in his hand the fire and the knife... and they both went together" <note>Genesis 22:6</note>. The end of the matter is suspended in the Fifth Gate <note>The gate representing the fifth Sephirah, Gevurah</note>, and there you will find the explanation for this *Sefirah* <term>Emanation (ספירה)</term>. And know that this attribute is also called "Place."

## Wealth
<note>original: "Ha-Osher" (העושר)</note>
Sometimes it is for good and sometimes for evil, for "wealth is kept for its owner to his own hurt" <note>Ecclesiastes 5:12</note>. From this place comes "red gold," and one only merits it through Judgment <note>original: "Din" (דין)</note>. From this attribute, the "Attribute of the *Teruah*" <note>original: "תרועה" (Teruah). One of the shofar sounds, characterized by broken, sobbing notes, associated with Divine Judgment.</note> is awakened when it battles against the enemies. However, on Rosh Hashanah <note>The Jewish New Year</note>, Abraham and Jacob surround it with a "plain" sound <note>original: "קול פשוט" (kol pashut). This refers to the Tekiah, the long, straight shofar blast associated with Mercy.</note>. Therefore, it is "plain before it and plain after it." "Happy is the people that know the *Teruah*" <note>Psalm 89:16</note>, for they participate in it and know how it operates. "Can a man walk upon coals and his feet not be scorched?" "Can a man take fire in his bosom and his clothes not be burned?" <note>Proverbs 6:27-28</note>. Therefore—

---

The wall of Jericho was swallowed up by means of the *Teruah*. We must awaken it on Rosh Hashanah through the Attribute of Mercy—Abraham and Jacob. "God has ascended with a *Teruah*, the Lord with the sound of a Shofar" <note>Psalm 47:6</note>. And therefore, "when you blow a *Teruah* a second time, they shall journey" <note>Numbers 10:6</note>. And it is written: "Rise up, O Lord, and let Your enemies be scattered." But "when the assembly is to be gathered, you shall blow, but you shall not sound a *Teruah*" <note>Numbers 10:7</note>. "When you go to war in your land... then you shall sound a *Teruah*" <note>Numbers 10:9</note>. "And on the day of your gladness and your festivals... you shall blow with the trumpets." And regarding Rosh Hashanah, even though it is the New Year, "it shall be a day of *Teruah* for you" <note>Numbers 29:1</note> "at the full moon, for our feast day" <note>Psalm 81:4</note>, because Abraham and Jacob surround it. And we conclude the prayer: "For You hear the sound of the Shofar... You have been from of old, and You listen to the *Teruah*, and there is none like You. Blessed are You, who hears the sound of the *Teruah* of His people Israel with mercy." Upon this judgment, contemplate the secret of the judgments bound to *Gevurah* <term>Strength/Judgment (גבורה)</term>. This *Sefirah* is also called:

## Arm
<note>original: "Zero'ah" (זרועה)</note>
For He wages wars and takes vengeance for Israel; that is to say, when Kingship <note>original: "Malchut" (מלכות). The tenth Sephirah, which represents God's sovereignty in the world.</note> clothes itself in the weaponry of *Gevurah*. "Awake, awake, put on strength, O Arm of the Lord, as in the ancient days, in the generations of old! Is it not You who hacked Rahab in pieces and pierced the dragon?" <note>Isaiah 51:9</note>. And it is written: "By the greatness of Your Arm they shall be as still as a stone" <note>Exodus 15:16</note>. And it is written: "The eternal God is a dwelling place, and underneath are the everlasting arms" <note>Deuteronomy 33:27</note>. Meaning, when he mentions both of them, he mentions "arms" [plural]. But when he mentions one "arm," it is the Left, for "Your right hand, and Your arm, and the light of Your countenance" <note>Psalm 44:4</note>. And there is a "clothing in the Arm" when Kingship clothes itself in the Arm of *Gevurah*. One who understands this secret will understand what our Sages of blessed memory said regarding Rabbi Hanina ben Dosa and his companions: "The entire world is sustained for his sake, yet he is sustained"
->**by the Arm**<-
->**of the Lord**<-

<summary>This page defines the titles "Rock," "Wealth," and "Arm" as symbols for the Sephirah of Gevurah (Judgment), explaining how the harshness of judgment is tempered by the mercy of the patriarchs during the Shofar blowing on Rosh Hashanah.</summary>
